The Technique's final damage is halved, rounding up. Air Slash therefore extends a melee Technique's reach, but reduces its damage. It does not grant a free new attack or remove any costs of the Technique used.

Sharpening

(1 ST)

1 time per turn. Before making an attack with a sword, you may spend 1 ST. For that single attack, treat the target's Passive Defence as reduced by 2. The modifier is not permanent and does not apply to allies' attacks. Example: if the target has Passive Defence 13, your attack with Sharpening treats it as 11.

d12 · Veteran

The Swordsman perfects the use of two blades, develops counterattacks and gains advanced weapon-drawing and weapon-defence techniques.

More Swords · Mastery

Prerequisite: More Swords · Improved · 1 time per turn. The extra attack with the second blade granted by More Swords · Improved may be a Swordsman Technique instead of a basic attack. The Talent does not add a third attack: it changes what you may use as the extra attack. The chosen Technique retains its requirements and any costs.

Counterblow

When you use a compatible Swordsman attack Technique as Active Defence and your defence result exceeds the enemy's attack roll, you also deal the normal Damage Die of the Technique used. A tie neutralises the attack but deals no damage. Counterblow does not grant an additional Reaction and does not automatically apply the Technique's other offensive effects, unless specific rules say otherwise. The Talent does not turn a normal Parry into an attack.

Lightning Draw

(2 ST)

1 time per combat. If you begin the combat with your blade still sheathed, you may spend 2 ST to make your first attack before normal Initiative. This attack is made with Advantage. The ability requires the blade to be sheathed at the start. It does not activate simply by sheathing it again during combat.

Riposte

(1 ST)

Prerequisite: Counterblow. After a successful Active Defence performed with a compatible Swordsman attack Technique, you may spend 1 ST to immediately make a basic attack with the same sword. Riposte grants a separate attack, with its own attack roll and the normal defences, even if Active Defence succeeded with a tie. A tie does not deal Counterblow damage. Riposte does not grant a general Action.

Indestructible Blade

(Passive) Your blade cannot normally be broken or damaged. It may also Parry weapons of a higher Grade. The Talent protects the blade and expands what it can parry, but does not mean that every Parry automatically succeeds.

d20 · Legend

Supreme Talent · Asura

Asura

(Ultimate)

5 ST

Cost

1×

Per combat

Requires the Game Master's permission. Asura concentrates all the Swordsman's ability into a single strike. To hit, make one roll consisting of:

Technique + Technique + Weapon Grade

The Technique Attribute die is therefore rolled twice and added to the weapon die. Damage depends on the margin achieved over the target's Passive Defence.

Asura Damage

Hit marginDamage
+0 / +33 Weapon Dice
+4 / +74 Weapon Dice
+8 / +115 Weapon Dice
+12 or more6 Weapon Dice

The Weapon Die is the Grade of the weapon used.

Asura exception: a margin of 0 still deals 3 Weapon Dice, as shown in the first band of its table. The normal rule that a tie is defended still applies to all other attacks.

Example · d12 Katana

A Swordsman has:

  • d20 Technique
  • d12 Katana

When using Asura, their attack roll is:

2d20 + d12

If they exceed the target's Passive Defence by a margin of at least +12, they deal:

6d12 damage

If instead they achieve a margin between +4 and +7, they deal 4d12 damage.

Asura's special rules

Asura operates exclusively according to its own rules. When using it, no other Techniques, Talents, Style Signature or weapon special effects apply, except as written in Asura's description.

In particular, Double Slash does not add an additional Damage Die, even when Asura reaches a margin of +4.

The damage increase based on the margin is already part of Asura's effect.

Progression · More Swords

More Swords is the stepped path that develops fighting with two blades.

VersionGradeEffect
Basicd8You may carry up to 2 swords, but fight with one at a time.
Improvedd10One free extra basic attack with the second blade, 1 time per turn.
Masteryd12The extra attack with the second blade may become a Swordsman Technique.

Later versions require the previous ones.

Improved and Mastery do not grant two separate extra attacks: Mastery upgrades the extra attack already obtained with Improved.

4.4 · Sniper

The Sniper is a Fighter specialising in ranged attacks. They study the battlefield, choose a favourable position and strike targets using range, precision and timing.

Their path allows them to progressively extend weapon range, overcome obstacles to aiming and interfere with enemy actions. At higher Grades, the Sniper can focus a sequence of shots on a single target through the Supreme Talent Lead Storm.

Style Sheet

CharacteristicValue
RoleFighter
StyleSniper
Role SkillObservation
Starting Graded8
SpecialisationRanged attacks
TraitThe Distance
SignatureExposed Point

Builder Effects

The Sniper may use only Dash, Laceration, Aim, Ricochet and Chain. The Technique's range comes from the weapon held; Long Shot and Prestige abilities may exceed it. Ammunition applies its own effects and is not part of the Builder. The Sniper has no ordinary Style Area Form.

Sniper Talents intended for ranged attacks do not automatically apply to melee attacks.

Trait · The Distance

Always active · Free

The Sniper specialises in ranged combat, outside melee, and in anticipating opponents.

The Trait identifies the Style's specialisation but does not itself grant a numerical Initiative bonus. Specific effects on range, aiming and timing are granted by acquired Talents.

Signature · Exposed Point

Always active · Free

When the Sniper scores a clean hit with a +4 margin, they identify a vulnerable point on the target.

Their next strike against the same enemy treats the target's Passive Defence as reduced by:

−2 Passive Defence

The reduction applies to the Sniper's next strike against that target. It is not a general penalty available to all allies unless the character acquires Hawk’s Eye.

Example: a Sniper scores a clean hit against an enemy with Passive Defence 13. Their next strike against the same opponent treats Passive Defence as 11.

Sniper Talent Tree

Role SkillTier
d8 ObservationDeckhand
d10 ObservationPirate
d12 ObservationVeteran
d20 ObservationLegend

Reaching the required Grade makes the Talents in the corresponding band available. Each Talent must still be acquired through normal Upgrade progression, meeting its prerequisites.

d8 · Deckhand

The initial Talents allow the Sniper to improve range, reloading, handling cover and preparing a shot.

Long Shot · Basic

Passive

Increase the effective range of your compatible ranged weapons by one band.

The Talent changes the weapon's range, not the character's Movement.

It does not grant a fixed increase in metres: use the range bands established by the equipment rules.

Rapid Reload

1 time per turn

Once per turn, reloading a weapon does not require the main Action: the Talent uses the Bonus Action instead, as established in §10.13.

The Talent removes the Action cost for one reload during the turn. It does not grant additional attacks or automatically remove any special weapon requirements.

Steady Eye

Passive

You ignore the target's partial cover.

Total cover still normally prevents you from hitting them.

Steady Eye therefore does not allow you to shoot through any obstacle: it removes only the benefit of partial cover.

Stakeout

No ST cost specified

If you spend an entire Action without moving to aim at a target, your next ranged attack against them gains Advantage.

The benefit ends when you attack, move or take damage before making the attack.

Example: a Sniper stays still and uses their Action to aim at a Marine officer. If they do not move or take damage before firing, their next ranged attack against that officer has Advantage.

d10 · Pirate

At this Grade, the Sniper gains tools to control opponents' movement and strike specific targets.

Long Shot · Improved

Passive

Prerequisite: Long Shot · Basic.

The effective range of your ranged weapons increases by another band.

This increase adds to the band already obtained with the Basic version.

Shot from the Shadows

No ST cost specified

If you are Hidden and unseen, your first ranged attack before revealing your position gains Advantage.

The Talent requires both conditions: being hidden and not being seen by the target.

It does not guarantee that the character remains hidden after the attack.

Targeted Shot

Precision attack

Instead of dealing damage, you may choose a specific target: a held weapon, a leg, a rope, a bolt or another plausible element.

The roll is made against:

Passive Defence +2

If it succeeds, apply the appropriate effect to the chosen target instead of damage.

The concrete consequence depends on what is hit and the situation. Targeted Shot does not also grant the attack's normal damage.

Example: a Sniper wants to strike the weapon held by an enemy with Passive Defence 11. They attack against a threshold of 13. If they succeed, they apply the appropriate effect to the chosen target instead of dealing damage.

Covering Fire

1 ST · Action

As an Action, spend 1 ST and choose a zone within your weapon's range.

Until the start of your next turn, the first enemy who moves in the open in that zone may be targeted with a ranged attack using your Reaction.

The Talent therefore requires preparation and an available Reaction. It does not produce an automatic attack against every enemy crossing the zone.

d12 · Veteran

Advanced Talents allow the Sniper to exploit extreme distances, hinder opponents' actions and achieve particularly complex trajectories.

Long Shot · Mastery

Passive

Prerequisite: Long Shot · Improved.

You may attack at extreme range, as far as a plausible line of sight exists.

This Talent represents the fullest development of the Long Shot path.

Line of sight remains a requirement: Mastery alone does not allow you to strike invisible targets or targets completely hidden behind obstacles.

Hawk’s Eye

Passive

When you score a clean hit with a +4 margin, the −2 Passive Defence reduction granted by the Exposed Point Signature becomes available to anyone attacking that target.

The effect lasts until the end of your next turn.

The normally personal Signature thus becomes an offensive support tool for the entire crew.

Anticipated Shot

1 time per turn · Reaction

When an enemy you are observing declares an Action, you may attack them as a Reaction, before they complete it.

If your attack hits, the enemy's declared Action is performed with Disadvantage.

Anticipated Shot does not automatically cancel the opponent's Action: it penalises its execution if the Sniper's attack succeeds.

Impossible Shot

2 ST

By spending 2 ST, you may attempt an attack with an unusual trajectory: a ricochet, a shot through a slit, an angle or a path that reaches a target outside direct line of sight.

A plausible path for the shot must exist.

The Talent does not make projectiles capable of freely passing through walls and solid obstacles.

Held Breath

2 ST · 1 time per combat

By spending 2 ST, you prepare your next attack.

If the attack hits, it is automatically considered a clean hit with a +4 margin.

This allows you to activate the Exposed Point Signature without naturally achieving the required margin.

The Talent does not turn a missed attack into a success.

d20 · Legend

Supreme Talent · Lead Storm

Lead Storm

Ultimate

5 ST

Cost

1×

Per combat

Requires the Game Master's permission.

Choose a single target and make three separate attack rolls.

Each roll uses:

Attribute + Weapon Grade

Each successful strike deals 2 Weapon Dice, for a combined maximum of 6 Weapon Dice.

HitsTotal damage
0None
12 Weapon Dice
24 Weapon Dice
36 Weapon Dice

Lightning-fast ammunition

Before each of the three shots, you may change projectile type, choosing any compatible ammunition you possess.

The change requires no Action or additional Stamina. The ammunition's effects apply normally.

Surgical precision

Each shot may be directed at a specific point on the target, without giving up the attack's 2 Weapon Dice.

Lead Storm's special rules

The Talent operates exclusively according to its own rules.

It does not apply other Techniques, Talents, the Style Signature or weapon special effects, except as expressly stated in Lead Storm's description.

Compatible ammunition effects are an explicitly provided exception and continue to apply.

The three shots are directed at a single target. They cannot freely be distributed among three different enemies.

Example

A Sniper uses Lead Storm with a d12 Grade rifle.

They make three separate attack rolls, each with Attribute + d12.

If all three hit, they deal a total of:

6d12 damage

Before each shot, they may select different compatible ammunition from what they possess, applying its effects normally.

The Exposed Point Signature does not activate during the sequence, since Lead Storm follows its own rules exclusively.

Progression · Long Shot

Each version requires the previous one. The increases from the first two versions are cumulative.

4.5 · Crusher

The Crusher is a Fighter specialising in blunt weapons. Maces, hammers, clubs and other impact weapons become tools for breaking down opponents' defences, moving enemies and destroying obstacles.

Their Style follows one principle: every strike must carry weight.

The Crusher may temporarily sacrifice their own defence to increase attack power, or hold a stable position and repel anyone trying to overwhelm them.

Style Sheet

Crusher

  • Fighter · Impact
  • Role Skill: Athletics
  • Starting Grade: d8
  • Equipment: compatible blunt weapons
  • Trait: The Weight of Iron
  • Signature: Devastating Impact

Builder Effects

The Crusher may use Unbalance, Breakthrough, Launch, Guard, Ally Shield, Fury, Crash and Charge. They may also build Close Area (d8, 2 ST): a circle centred on the Crusher, with a radius equal to the blunt weapon's effective Reach and no Range. Wider areas require a Source or special ability.

Crusher Style Techniques may be used only while the character holds and uses a compatible blunt weapon.

They do not apply to bare-handed strikes or attacks with incompatible weapons.

Trait · The Weight of Iron

Always active · Free

The Crusher knows how to use the inertia of blunt weapons, such as maces, hammers, clubs, knuckledusters and poles used for impact strikes.

High-Grade blunt weapons may unlock their special effects in the Crusher's hands.

The equipment's actual compatibility is still determined by the Arsenal rules.

The Trait does not automatically increase the Damage Die: specific increases are granted by owned Talents and Techniques.

Signature · Devastating Impact

Always active · Free

When you score a clean hit with a +4 margin using a blunt weapon, the target is:

Pushed 3 metres

Signature effect

Devastating Impact does not require acquiring a Talent or incur an additional Stamina cost.

The push does not automatically cause the Off-Balance condition.

Crusher Talent Tree

The Crusher's reference Skill is Athletics.

AthleticsTier
d8Deckhand
d10Pirate
d12Veteran
d20Legend

Talents become available when Athletics reaches the required Grade. They must still be acquired through Upgrades, meeting any prerequisites.

d8 · Deckhand

Heavy Strike · Basic

2 ST · 1 time per turn

When attacking with a blunt weapon, you may spend 2 ST to add:

+1 Damage Die

In exchange, your Passive Defence decreases by 3 from the moment you declare the attack until the start of your next turn.

The penalty applies even if the attack misses.

Example: a Crusher with Passive Defence 11 declares Heavy Strike. Their Passive Defence drops to 8 for the stated duration, regardless of the strike's result.

Planted Feet

Passive

As long as you do not move voluntarily, enemies' attempts to Push you or make you Off-Balance are made with Disadvantage.

The Talent does not automatically prevent displacement: it makes success harder.

Rebound Blow

1 ST · Reaction · 1 time per turn

When an enemy misses you with a melee attack, you may spend 1 ST and use your Reaction to make a basic attack with a blunt weapon.

The Talent requires the enemy's attack to fail. It does not activate when you are hit.

Demolisher

Passive

Your blunt-weapon strikes deal full damage to doors, walls, structures and objects.

You ignore reductions due to the materials' simple hardness.

This effect concerns material obstacles and does not automatically remove creatures' damage reductions.

d10 · Pirate

Heavy Strike · Improved

2 ST · 1 time per turn

Prerequisite: Heavy Strike · Basic.

When attacking with a blunt weapon, you may spend 2 ST to gain both:

  • +1 Damage Die.
  • Advantage on the attack roll.

Your Passive Defence decreases by 3 from declaring the attack until the start of your next turn, even if you miss.

This version replaces Heavy Strike · Basic. You cannot combine them to gain two additional Damage Dice.

Shock Wave

2 ST

When you hit an enemy with a blunt weapon, you may spend 2 ST and choose a second opponent within 3 metres of the main target.

The second enemy is pushed 3 metres.

Shock Wave does not automatically grant additional damage to the second target: its stated effect is the push.

Guard Breaker

1 time per turn

When you hit an enemy with a blunt weapon, their next Parry or Active Defence, performed before the end of their next turn, is made with Disadvantage.

The Talent does not reduce the target's Passive Defence.

Counterweight

Reaction · 1 time per turn

When you successfully resist an attempt to Push you or make you Off-Balance, you may use your Reaction to push the opponent who attempted it 3 metres.

The ability requires successful resistance. It does not activate if the enemy succeeds in moving you or making you Off-Balance.

d12 · Veteran

Heavy Strike · Mastery

2 ST · 1 time per turn

Prerequisite: Heavy Strike · Improved.

When using this version, you gain:

  • Advantage on the attack roll.
  • +2 Damage Dice.
  • If the strike hits, the target falls Off-Balance.

In exchange, your Passive Defence decreases by 5 until your next turn.

This version replaces the previous ones: you do not add together the Basic, Improved and Mastery bonuses.

Example

A Crusher has Passive Defence 13 and uses Heavy Strike · Mastery.

They spend 2 ST, make the roll with Advantage and gain +2 Damage Dice.

Their Passive Defence drops to 8 for the stated duration.

If they hit, the target also falls Off-Balance. If they miss, the defensive penalty remains.

Chain Impact

Shock Wave upgrade

Prerequisite: Shock Wave.

When using Shock Wave, you may affect up to 3 additional enemies within 3 metres of the main target.

All affected additional enemies are pushed 3 metres.

Chain Impact expands the maximum number of secondary targets without automatically increasing damage.

Counterblow

Passive. When you use a compatible Crusher attack Technique as Active Defence and your defence result exceeds the enemy's attack roll, you also deal the normal Damage Die of the Technique used. A tie neutralises the attack but deals no damage. Counterblow does not grant an additional Reaction and does not automatically apply the Technique's other offensive effects, unless specific rules say otherwise. The Technique must be compatible with the blunt weapon held.

Absolute Centre of Gravity

Passive

Prerequisite: Planted Feet.

As long as you meet all the following conditions, you cannot be Pushed or made Off-Balance by physical effects:

  • You remain conscious.
  • You are in contact with the ground.
  • You do not move voluntarily.

If any of these conditions ceases to apply, the Talent's protection no longer applies.

Absolute Centre of Gravity represents the perfection of Planted Feet: it does not grant general immunity to any effect or condition.

Interception Blow

Reaction · 1 time per turn

When an enemy enters your reach, you may use your Reaction to make a basic attack with a blunt weapon.

The Talent allows you to intercept an approaching opponent, but does not grant additional Reactions.

d20 · Legend

Supreme Talent · Cataclysm

Cataclysm

Ultimate

5 ST

Cost

1×

Per combat

Requires the Game Master's permission.

Cataclysm is a devastating attack consisting of two consecutive resolutions: a direct impact and a shock wave.

First resolution · Impact

Make an attack against a single target using:

Attribute + Weapon Grade

If the roll hits, the target takes:

4 Weapon Dice

Second resolution · Shock Wave

Make a new roll:

Attribute + Weapon Grade

Compare the result with the Passive Defence of all targets within 5 metres of the impact point, including the main target.

Each target hit takes:

2 Weapon Dice

They are also pushed 5 metres and fall Off-Balance.

Total damage

TargetDamage
Main target, hit only by the Impact4 Weapon Dice
Target hit by the Shock Wave2 Weapon Dice
Main target, hit by both resolutions6 Weapon Dice

Cataclysm also devastates the surrounding area. Walls, doors, floors, cover and other structures suffer the impact's consequences as appropriate to the situation.

Cataclysm's special rules

Cataclysm operates exclusively according to its own rules. It does not apply other Techniques, Talents, the Style Signature or weapon effects, except as stated in its description.

The first roll concerns a single target. The second is compared separately with the Passive Defence of all targets in the stated area.

The main target is included in the Shock Wave: they may therefore suffer both effects, provided both rolls hit them.

Example · d12 Hammer

A Crusher uses Cataclysm with a d12 Grade hammer.

The first attack hits the main target, dealing 4d12 damage.

The second roll hits the same target again and strikes two other enemies within 5 metres of the impact point.

The main target takes another 2d12 damage, for a total of 6d12. The other two enemies take 2d12 damage each.

All targets hit by the Shock Wave are also pushed 5 metres and fall Off-Balance.

Progression · Heavy Strike

Heavy Strike is the stepped Talent that defines the Crusher's main offensive trade-off.

VersionBenefitCostDefensive penalty
Basic · d8+1 Damage Die2 STPD −3
Improved · d10+1 Damage Die, Advantage2 STPD −3
Mastery · d12+2 Damage Dice, Advantage, Off-Balance on a hit2 STPD −5

All three versions may be used once per turn.

Each higher version requires and replaces the previous one. The defensive penalties do not stack.

The penalty takes effect when the Crusher declares Heavy Strike and still applies if the attack misses.

4.6 · Doctor

The Doctor is the crew specialist concerned with health, substances and their applications. They can save a badly injured companion, develop antidotes or turn raw reagents into preparations usable in combat.

When choosing Doctor, you must select one of its three Styles.

Role Sheet

All three Styles use Medicine to access their Talents.

The Skill's Grade determines which Talents may be acquired. Each Talent must still be obtained through Upgrades, meeting any prerequisites.

4.6.1 · Infirmary

Infirmary is the Style dedicated to healing and recovery. Its specialist can stabilise the injured, restore HP and Stamina, perform operations and improve recovery for the entire crew.

Trait · The Ward

Always active · Free

A Doctor specialising in Infirmary has the expertise required to perform true Field Treatments.

A character with Medicine may provide first aid, diagnose an injury, stabilise an injured person and use medical tools and preparations normally.

The ability to directly restore HP through Field Treatment, however, belongs to Infirmary and follows the rules below.

Field Treatment

Field Treatment represents a quick medical intervention to restore an injured character to operational condition.

  • Activation: Action.
  • Target: a character you can reach and physically treat.
  • Check: Cunning + Medicine.
  • Ordinary Threshold: 8.
  • Materials: appropriate medical supplies.

To perform Field Treatment, you must physically reach the patient and use the necessary tools. A wall, a closed barrier or another obstacle physically preventing the intervention makes Treatment impossible.

Roll: Cunning + Medicine against Threshold 8.

If the Check succeeds, make a full Medicine roll. The result indicates how many HP the patient recovers.

The roll used to determine recovered HP is not a second success Check: it only quantifies the healing.

Recovery cannot bring the character above their Maximum HP.

Each Field Treatment consumes the dose, preparation or expendable medical supply required for the intervention. A Kit or permanent tool is not consumed, unless its own sheet specifies otherwise.

During a combat, a given character may normally benefit from only one Field Treatment.

A character at 0 HP may receive Field Treatment. If the intervention restores at least 1 HP, they return above 0 HP and regain consciousness.

Cyborgs do not normally recover HP through Medicine and follow the specific rules of the Cyborg Manual.

Signature · The Steady Hand

Always active · Free

When you achieve a margin of at least +4 on the Medicine Check of a successful Field Treatment, the patient also receives both of the following benefits:

  • Recovers 1d6 ST.
  • Removes one removable negative Condition of their choice.

The Signature uses the margin of the Field Treatment Check, not the subsequent roll that determines recovered HP.

Condition removal follows the Condition and Condition Cause rules in Chapter 6.

d8 Talents · Deckhand

Healing Herbs

When searching for medical ingredients, add Medicine to the Gathering roll: Cunning + Exploration + Medicine.

Resuscitation Specialist

When you successfully stabilise a companion at 0 HP, you also restore 1d4 HP to them.

If recovery brings them above 0 HP, the companion regains consciousness.

This intervention remains Stabilisation and does not count as Field Treatment; it therefore does not consume the patient's normal Field Treatment limit.

Surgeon’s Hands · Basic

When your Field Treatment succeeds, add one additional Medicine die to the HP restored.

The additional die modifies only HP recovery: it is not added to the Cunning + Medicine Check used to determine whether the Treatment succeeds.

Diagnosis

As an Action, study a living enemy and make a Medicine check against their Passive Defence. If you succeed, the GM reveals a weakness; the first ally to exploit it in the same battle rolls with Advantage.

d10 Talents · Pirate

Expert Recovery

When your Field Treatment succeeds, the patient also recovers 1d6 ST.

Stamina recovery applies after normally resolving the HP restored by the Treatment.

Constant Care

The same character can benefit from your Field Treatment twice per combat instead of once.

Each operation normally requires its own Action, Check and necessary materials.

Surgeon’s Hands · Improved

Prerequisite: Surgeon’s Hands · Basic

With the same Action, you can perform Field Treatment on two companions you can physically reach.

Resolve the Field Treatment Check and HP recovery separately for each patient.

The necessary materials must be available for both.

Each patient normally uses one of their Field Treatment uses for the battle.

Immediate Intervention

Reaction · 1 time per combat

When a companion still alive within 5 metres reaches 0 HP, you can use your Reaction to reach them and stabilise them immediately.

The necessary displacement, up to a maximum of 5 metres, is part of the Reaction and does not use your normal Movement.

The route must, however, actually be passable.

You must also be able to physically reach the patient and have sufficient access to perform the operation.

Immediate Intervention is not healing at a distance and does not allow passage through walls, closed doors, impassable grilles or other obstacles that physically prevent treatment.

If you possess Resuscitation Specialist, its recovery of 1d4 HP applies normally.

This Stabilisation does not consume the patient's normal Field Treatment limit.

d12 Talents · Veteran

Surgeon’s Hands · Mastery

Prerequisite: Surgeon’s Hands · Improved

When your Field Treatment succeeds, you can also remove one removable negative Status from the patient.

The benefit adds to the previous versions of Surgeon’s Hands.

Removing the Status does not automatically eliminate a physical, environmental or chemical cause that continues to apply it.

Pressure Points

1 time per combat, when you hit a living creature in melee, you can inflict Off-Balance in addition to damage. Requires Diagnosis.

Reading the Breath

Without a roll or Action, recognise the general level of injury of a visible enemy. When they are about to fall, the first ally to attack them that round has Advantage.

Operating Theatre

With sufficient time, adequate light and a clean surface, you can treat physical Persistent Consequences requiring genuine medical intervention, such as:

  • Fractures.
  • Embedded bullets or foreign bodies.
  • Deep wounds.
  • Injuries that cannot be resolved by normal Field Treatment.

If the operation's outcome is uncertain, make a Cunning + Medicine check against the Threshold specified by the Consequence.

If the Consequence has no Threshold of its own, the Game Master uses the normal difficulty Thresholds in Chapter 5.

A success removes or improves the Consequence according to its description.

Operating Theatre does not automatically regenerate lost body parts or eliminate diseases, poisons or other conditions requiring different treatment.

Ship’s Doctor

While aboard the ship and in good health, a Short Rest under your supervision doubles the crew's ordinary recovery: HP and ST return to their respective maxima, as with a Long Rest's recovery. This benefit modifies recovery; it does not turn the Short Rest into a Long Rest for abilities expressly requiring the latter. After recovery, those who rested under your supervision also receive 2 additional ST. These can take Current Stamina above the normal maximum and can be spent normally; they do not permanently increase Maximum Stamina.

d20 Supreme Talent · No One Dies Under My Care

No One Dies Under My Care

  • Ultimate
  • Cost: 5 ST
  • Frequency: 1 time per scene

All allies within 10 metres recover HP equal to twice their Hit Die and are freed from all removable negative Statuses.

A companion at 0 HP gets up with half their Maximum HP.

The Ultimate does not automatically destroy physical restraints, neutralise substances still active or remove Persistent Consequences.

Requirements: the Doctor's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

4.6.2 · Toxicologist

The Toxicologist studies poisons, sedatives and antidotes. They can use substances to weaken enemies, neutralise poisoning and prepare the crew for known threats.

Trait · The Laboratory

Always active · Free

The Toxicologist has specialised expertise in preparing, identifying and using poisons, sedatives and antidotes.

They can extract toxic substances, prepare usable doses and recognise how to administer them correctly.

The Toxicologist's substances do not produce effects freely chosen at the moment of use: each poison, sedative or antidote has its own sheet establishing its Threshold, effect, duration, exposure route and treatment.

Doses and exposure routes

Poisons and sedatives are stored and used in doses.

Each dose has an exposure route stated in its sheet:

  • Wound — must enter the body through a weapon, needle, dart or another means capable of wounding the target.
  • Contact — must directly reach skin, mucous membranes or a surface specified by the substance.
  • Inhalation — the target must breathe the contaminated air.
  • Ingestion — the target must ingest the substance.

A dose cannot be used through a different route unless its sheet allows it.

A substance's sheet must specify at least:

Name · Category · Exposure route · Threshold · Effect · Duration · Antidote or treatment.

Applying a toxin

Preparing a dose for administration through a weapon, projectile, dart, food or another carrier normally requires:

Action · Cunning + Medicine · Threshold 8

On a success, the dose is correctly applied and ready to use.

On a failure, the dose is contaminated, dispersed or ineffectively applied and is consumed.

This is the Medicine roll considered by The Right Dose effect.

The Medicine roll does not replace any attack needed to hit the target or the Save against the substance.

If the toxin is applied to a weapon or projectile, the subsequent attack is resolved normally. If it hits and the exposure route is compatible, the target makes the toxin's specified Save.

A missed attack does not automatically consume the dose applied to the weapon or recoverable projectile. The dose remains available until actually administered or until the end of the scene.

When a dose is actually administered, it is consumed regardless of the Save's outcome.

Toxin potency and duration

Every poison or sedative has its own Threshold.

The Threshold recorded in the substance's sheet is a specific rule and takes precedence over the general table of graded sources. Effective Sedatives, Refined Toxins and similar abilities continue to modify it according to their own text.

Saves use the Attribute specified by the substance. For a normal poison introduced into the body, unless otherwise specified, use Strength against the poison's Threshold, as provided by the Poisoned Status rules.

Effects with a numerical duration must state it in turns, rounds or another precise unit.

A duration expressed as ‘until an antidote’, ‘for the scene’ or another condition is not considered a numerical duration.

Repeated applications of the same substance do not automatically stack the same effect: they normally refresh its duration.

Reductions to the same Attribute do not stack. If multiple effects reduce the same Attribute, apply the largest reduction.

No toxin-induced reduction can take an Attribute below d4, unless an ability expressly states otherwise.

Clouds, obstacles and the environment

A cloud occupies a real volume.

Walls and closed doors block its spread.

It can reach different floors only through spaces that are actually connected and within its radius.

Strong wind or sufficiently powerful ventilation can disperse the cloud before its specified duration; under clearly sufficient conditions, the Game Master can end it at the end of the current round.

A normal cloud dispersed in air does not work underwater, unless the substance used expressly states otherwise.

Protection against inhaled substances

An effective Filtering Mask prevents exposure to substances acting exclusively through Inhalation while properly worn and with a working filter.

Simple respiratory protection does not automatically prevent Blinded caused by smoke, irritation or obscured sight.

Protecting the eyes as well requires full protection expressly stating it.

Poison immunity prevents Poisoned and the substance's other toxic effects, but does not automatically grant immunity to a cloud's visual obstruction.

Signature · The Right Dose

Always active · Free

When you obtain a margin of at least +4 on the Medicine Check used to apply a toxin or sedative, you prepare that single dose with particular precision.

If the dose subsequently reaches a target through the correct exposure route, the first Save made against that dose is made with Disadvantage.

The Right Dose does not modify the substance's effects, add Statuses, increase duration or reduce Attributes.

If the dose is not administered before the scene ends, the Signature's benefit ends.

d8 Talents · Deckhand

Poison Hunter

You gain Advantage on any poison extraction.

Effective Sedatives

The Threshold of Saves against your sedatives increases by +2.

The benefit applies only to substances classified as Sedatives.

Refined Toxins · Basic

The Threshold of Saves against your poisons increases by +2.

Dipped Blade

Bonus Action

You use one dose of your toxin to coat a weapon, ammunition or another compatible carrier.

You normally make the application Check of Cunning + Medicine against Threshold 8, but the operation uses the Bonus Action instead of the Action.

A Technique can be poisoned only if actually executed through a weapon, projectile, needle or another physical means capable of administering the substance.

A punch, shock wave, ranged slash or another Technique without contact with the toxin cannot be poisoned merely because it is called a Technique.

The first compatible hit that lands exposes the target to the toxin and consumes the dose.

If the hit misses, the dose remains on the carrier.

You can keep at most two dipped carriers ready simultaneously per combat.

d10 Talents · Pirate

Antidote Master

Halve the Threshold needed to prepare the antidote to a poison you have identified.

Identifying the substance and preparing its antidote remain two distinct steps.

Acquired Immunity

You are immune to the effects of substances classified as Poison.

You can identify one by taste and handle poisons without suffering their effects.

This immunity does not automatically include:

  • Sedatives not also classified as Poison.
  • Acids and corrosive substances.
  • Diseases.
  • Gases producing purely physical effects or obscuring vision.
  • Medicines and beneficial preparations.

If a substance also has the Poison category, immunity applies to the toxic component specified in its sheet.

Refined Toxins · Improved

Prerequisite: Refined Toxins · Basic

Double the numerical duration of your poisons.

A duration of 2 turns becomes 4 turns; a duration of 3 turns becomes 6.

The Talent does not modify durations expressed as ‘for the scene’, ‘until an antidote is administered’ or other non-numerical conditions.

Sleep Dart

Action · 1 time per combat · Reach 10 m

Use a specially prepared dart and consume a dose of Sedative compatible with Wound exposure.

The dart and the means needed to launch it are part of using the Talent: a separate weapon is not required.

You must have line of fire and a physically clear trajectory to the target.

You do not make a separate attack roll: the target directly makes a Strength Save against the Sedative's Threshold.

If they fail, they become Asleep for a maximum of 10 rounds or until they take damage.

During this duration, they do not automatically repeat the Save at the end of every turn: the Talent's specific rule takes precedence over the Status's general procedure.

The dose is consumed even if the target succeeds on the Save.

Effective Sedatives normally increases the Threshold.

d12 Talents · Veteran

Refined Toxins · Mastery

Prerequisite: Refined Toxins · Improved

When a target fails their Save against your poison, they also lose one step in an Attribute of your choice until receiving an effective antidote.

The Attribute cannot fall below d4.

Repeated applications do not stack further reductions on the same Attribute.

Toxic Cloud

Action · 3 ST

Use and consume a prepared dose of Poison compatible with Inhalation exposure.

The Action includes drawing the container and dispersing its contents using a vial, bomb, nebuliser or another appropriate means.

No attack roll is required.

Choose a point you can see within 10 metres that the substance can physically reach. Walls, closed doors and other solid obstacles prevent placing the centre beyond them.

The chosen point generates a cloud with a 5-metre radius lasting 3 turns.

When the cloud appears, all creatures already in the area immediately make a Save.

Subsequently, a creature makes the same Save when:

  • It enters the cloud for the first time during its turn.
  • It starts its turn inside it.

The Save uses Strength against the consumed poison's Threshold, normally modified by your Talents.

If it fails:

  • It becomes Poisoned for the poison's normal duration.
  • It becomes Blinded while remaining inside the cloud.

Leaving the area ends the Blindness caused by the cloud, but does not automatically remove Poisoned.

Toxic Cloud uses the consumed dose's Threshold, but does not automatically copy any further special effects of that poison.

The dose is consumed when dispersed.

Finishing Blow

Against an already Poisoned target, your attacks deal +1 damage die. If the hit would bring them below half HP, they also suffer Off-Balance. Requires Dipped Blade.

Instant Antidote

Reaction · 1 time per combat · Reach 5 m

When a companion within 5 metres is exposed to poison or suffers a Status caused by a substance you have identified, you can use your Reaction to reach them and immediately administer an appropriate antidote.

You must possess an already prepared antidote dose.

The necessary displacement of up to 5 metres is part of the Reaction, but the route must be passable and you must be able to reach the target physically.

The dose is consumed.

The antidote neutralises the substance it was prepared for and removes Statuses directly caused by it.

Instant Antidote does not create an antidote at the moment of use.

Prophylaxis

Before a mission, you can prepare the crew against a poison or disease you have already identified.

Each treated character requires one dose of the appropriate prophylaxis.

Preparation, materials and cost follow the corresponding antidote or treatment design.

Treatment must be performed before exposure and cannot be improvised retroactively.

For the entire adventure, treated characters are immune to the specific chosen substance or disease.

Prophylaxis does not protect against other poisons, diseases or environmental effects not caused by the treated substance.

d20 Supreme Talent · Black Tide

Black Tide

  • Ultimate
  • Activation: Action
  • Cost: 5 ST
  • Frequency: 1 time per scene

By consuming a prepared dose of Poison compatible with Inhalation, you release an extraordinary quantity of toxin into the surrounding air.

The substance spreads from your position in a 15-metre radius.

Black Tide is an instantaneous dispersal, not a Persistent Zone.

Walls, closed doors and other solid obstacles prevent the substance from reaching isolated spaces.

All exposed creatures make a Strength Save against the Threshold of the poison used.

Anyone who fails:

  • Becomes Poisoned.
  • Becomes Off-Balance.
  • Loses one step in an Attribute of your choice for the rest of the scene.

The reduction cannot take the Attribute below d4 and does not stack with other reductions to the same Attribute.

Characters previously treated with Prophylaxis against that specific toxin are immune.

Other allies make the Save just like enemies.

Acquired Immunity protects the Toxicologist from the toxic effects of their own Black Tide, but grants no protection to other characters.

Requirements: the Toxicologist's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

4.6.3 · Chemist

The Chemist turns raw reagents into incendiary, acid, smoke and explosive preparations. They can produce substances for field use, modify their effects and develop a true production and trading business.

Trait · The Distillery

Always active · Free

The Chemist possesses the skills needed to turn raw reagents into usable or saleable preparations.

Each preparation is made through an already defined sheet.

The Threshold used to produce it measures preparation difficulty and does not automatically determine the preparation's damage, area or combat potency.

These values must be specified in its sheet before production.

Preparation sheet

Every preparation usable in play must list:

  • Name.
  • Family — Incendiary, Acid, Smoke, Explosive or another specified category.
  • Use — Target, Area, Contact, Charge or other.
  • Reach.
  • Initial damage, if present.
  • Area, if present.
  • Save, if present.
  • Effect or Status.
  • Duration.
  • Preparation Threshold.
  • Preparation time.
  • Materials.
  • Doses produced.

A preparation does not derive combat values from its manufacturing Threshold.

If a Catalogue entry gives only descriptions such as ‘damage in a small area’ or ‘major structural damage’, it must be completed with numerical values before use.

Using a preparation

Using a preparation normally consumes one dose.

Unless stated otherwise, it requires an Action.

A throwable preparation has an ordinary reach of 10 metres.

Direct-target preparation: make a Technique + Medicine check against the target's Passive Defence, unless the sheet specifies a different formula.

If the attack misses, the thrown dose is still consumed.

Area preparation: choose a visible, reachable point within Reach. You do not make an attack roll; targets in the Area instead resolve the Save specified by the preparation.

Walls and solid obstacles normally block an Area's spread.

All creatures in the Area are affected, including allies, unless an ability expressly allows them to be excluded.

Signature · The Reaction Spreads

Always active · Free

When you obtain a margin of at least +4 on a preparation's preparation Check, choose one of the following benefits.

Spread. If the preparation was offensive and intended for one target, it becomes an Area preparation with a circle of 3-metre radius.

The origin point remains the one normally specified by the preparation.

Having become an Area effect:

  • Targets take half the normal damage.
  • Statuses and other effects providing for it grant the normal Save.

Spread does not apply to preparations already covering an Area, antidotes, tonics, contact tools or charges to be placed.

Additional Production. Gain a second identical dose of the preparation without consuming additional ingredients.

The additional dose is produced at the same time as the original dose and has the same sheet.

The choice is made at the end of the preparation Check.

d8 Talents · Deckhand

Reactive Reagents

You can produce the reserved preparation families: incendiary, acid, smoke and explosive.

Improvisation

You can replace a missing ingredient with a consistent makeshift material.

The preparation Threshold increases by +2.

The substitute material is consumed normally.

Improvisation does not allow unique components or ingredients declared indispensable by the design to be replaced.

Violent Reactions · Basic

When your offensive preparation has a base Damage Die, add one further die of the same type to its initial damage.

Example: 1d8 preparation → 2d8.

The Talent does not create damage on a preparation without a base Damage Die.

Precise Throw

The reach of your throwable preparations increases from 10 to 15 metres.

When using an Area preparation, you can exclude a number of creatures equal to half the maximum value of your Medicine die, rounded down.

Example:

  • Medicine d8 → up to 4 creatures excluded.
  • Medicine d12 → up to 6.

The Talent modifies reach and target selection; it does not add an attack roll to a preparation normally using only a Save.

d10 Talents · Pirate

Efficient Distiller

Reduce the time needed to prepare a dose by 25%, rounding up to the work unit used.

You can also work on two preparations simultaneously in the same work block.

Each preparation:

  • Requires its own materials.
  • Retains its own Threshold.
  • Makes its preparation Check separately.

If the two preparations take different times, the block ends when the longer one is complete.

On Commission

You can organise production of identical preparations in batches.

A batch can contain up to 3 doses of the same preparation.

You must possess and consume the full materials for each dose.

The total time is:

  • 1 dose → normal time.
  • 2 doses → ×1.5 normal time.
  • 3 doses → ×2 normal time.

Make a separate preparation Check for each dose.

When selling personally produced preparations in a market with real demand, their commercial value increases by 20% before normal negotiation.

The Talent does not automatically guarantee buyers or remove normal market conditions.

Violent Reactions · Improved

Prerequisite: Violent Reactions · Basic

Double the radius of your preparations that already have an Area.

A 3-metre radius becomes 6 metres.

A preparation without an Area does not automatically gain one.

If The Reaction Spreads has turned a single-target preparation into a 3-metre-radius Area, Violent Reactions · Improved can increase it to 6 metres.

The Talent does not double throwing Reach.

Mix on the Fly

Action · 2 ST · maximum 2 times per combat

Choose a preparation you know and possess all the ingredients needed for one dose of.

Immediately consume the materials and make the normal preparation Check against the preparation's Threshold.

Success: produce a dose with all the characteristics already recorded in its sheet.

Failure: the dose is not produced; materials and ST remain consumed.

Mix on the Fly replaces normal preparation time, but does not modify the preparation's Threshold, ingredients or statistics.

The Action used produces the dose: it does not automatically use it.

To throw, apply or place it, you must subsequently use the normally required Action.

The dose must be used before the end of the scene.

d12 Talents · Veteran

Violent Reactions · Mastery

Prerequisite: Violent Reactions · Improved

Your offensive preparations also apply an additional effect linked to their family.

  • Incendiary — Burn. The target loses 1d4 HP at the start of their next 2 turns. They can use an Action and an appropriate means to extinguish the flames. Repeated applications refresh the duration but do not stack periodic damage.
  • Acid — Corrosion. Passive Defence −2 for 2 turns. Multiple applications do not stack the penalty. Suitable neutralisation can end it early.
  • Smoke — Blinded. The target becomes Blinded until the end of their next turn. If the preparation creates a persistent smoke zone, Blinded instead lasts while the target remains in the zone, according to the preparation's sheet.
  • Explosive — Off-Balance. The target suffers Off-Balance according to the normal Status rules.

When the preparation already grants a Save, that same Save also determines application of the Mastery effect.

If it has no Save, use the one provided by the applied Status.

Timed Trigger

You can prepare and arm explosive charges with different activation systems.

Placing and arming a charge: Action.

When placing it, choose one of the following triggers:

  • Timer — establish when it will detonate. In combat, state the exact round.
  • Contact — detonates when pressed, struck or activated according to the established method.
  • Pull — detonates when a wire, safety pin or connection is removed.
  • Manual — you can detonate it using your Reaction while within 30 metres and while the detonator remains functional.

Programming the trigger is part of the same Action used to place the charge.

You can keep a maximum of two charges armed simultaneously.

A creature physically reaching a charge can attempt to disarm it using an Action and an appropriate Cunning + Mechanics or Cunning + Medicine Check, depending on trigger type, against the charge's preparation Threshold.

A failure leaves the charge active, unless the operation physically activated its trigger.

Breaching Charge

When placing an explosive directly against a structure, you can use it as a Breaching Charge.

Against a structure with Structure Points, the charge deals its normal structural damage, and SP are reduced normally.

During a naval battle, you can instead place it directly on a reachable Module of the enemy ship.

In this case, when placing it choose whether to strike:

  • The hull — damage is normally deducted from the ship's SP.
  • The Module — damage is not also deducted from the ship's SP. If the charge produces at least 1 effective damage after any protection, an Intact Module becomes Damaged. An already Damaged Module becomes Destroyed.

A single Breaching Charge does not automatically destroy an Intact Module.

Mobile Workshop

You can produce preparations even without a distillery, working in improvised locations without penalties.

Powder Merchant

You have port contacts to obtain rare reagents and find buyers for your batches, including illegal ones. Requires On Commission.

d20 Supreme Talent · Chain Reaction

Chain Reaction

  • Ultimate
  • Activation: Action
  • Cost: 5 ST
  • Frequency: 1 time per scene

Choose up to 5 of your detonatable preparations already placed and still present within 15 metres of you.

They must be explosives, incendiaries or other preparations whose sheets actually allow detonation.

Antidotes, tonics, simple acid doses, spent smoke preparations and other objects without a compatible trigger cannot be chosen.

All chosen preparations detonate simultaneously from their actual positions.

Each preparation retains:

  • Its own damage.
  • Its own Area.
  • Its own Threshold.
  • Its own Statuses.
  • Its own effects.

There is no single Save for the entire Ultimate: each affected creature resolves the normal defences and Saves of preparations reaching it.

A creature can be reached by multiple detonations and take their respective damage. Copies of the same Status do not stack beyond the normal rules.

Allies in the Areas are hit normally. Chain Reaction does not automatically distinguish friends from enemies.

The ability does not place new preparations or create doses for free: everything that explodes must have actually been prepared and distributed before activation.

Requirements: the Chemist's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

4.7 · Cook

A crew can survive a battle, but without supplies and proper meals it will not get far. The Cook turns ingredients gathered during the voyage into dishes capable of restoring energy and preparing companions for the challenges ahead.

Their specialisation is not cooking during a battle, but preparing, preserving and serving food at the right moment.

Role Sheet

CharacteristicValue
RoleCook
Role SkillCooking
Starting Graded8
TraitThe Heart and the Engine
SignatureThe Pinnacle
PathSingle

The Cook uses Cooking as the reference Skill for accessing their Talents.

Trait · The Heart and the Engine

Free · Always active

The Cook is the specialist who enables the crew to tackle particularly complex food preparations.

They can make the Catalogue's ambitious dishes, with Threshold 10–12, which are not accessible to those without this specialisation.

The Trait does not guarantee successful preparation: the normal Cooking roll must still be made to determine the outcome.

Signature · The Pinnacle

Free · Always active

When you achieve a Critical with margin +4 on a Cooking roll, the dish becomes a masterpiece.

The dish performs at its best, and its Catalogue Category Bonus also activates.

The Signature is tied to preparing dishes, not attacks made by the character.

Talent Tree

Cooking GradeTier
d8Deckhand
d10Pirate
d12Veteran
d20Legend

Talents become available when Cooking reaches the required Grade, but must be acquired through Upgrades.

d8 · Deckhand

The first Talents improve ingredient gathering, reduce the consequences of failed preparations and allow you to serve a meal during a fight.

Cook’s Pantry

Passive

When you search or fish for ingredients, add your Cooking Die to the roll.

This benefit applies to obtaining ingredients, not to every exploration Check.

Never on an Empty Stomach

Passive

Your dishes never fail completely.

When a preparation would fail, the dish still produces the effects of a Strained result.

This does not turn the failure into a Critical result or automatically trigger the category Bonus.

Cook’s Hand · Basic

Passive

Your dishes restore +1 die of the resource they normally recover.

If a dish restores HP, add one die to its HP recovery. If it restores Stamina, add one die to its Stamina recovery.

The Talent does not automatically turn a dish that restores HP into one that also restores ST.

If a recipe restores both HP and ST, choose one of the two resources when preparing it: Cook’s Hand · Basic adds its die only to that resource.

Battlefield Service

Action · Conscious target within 5 metres

Serve an already prepared portion to a conscious companion within 5 metres.

Delivery and consumption are part of the Cook's same Action.

The recipient does not spend their own Action.

This ability does not include any Displacement by the Cook: the target must already be within 5 metres.

There must also be a physically possible way to deliver the food; a closed wall or another impassable obstacle prevents service.

The recipient must be conscious and physically able to eat or drink.

An Unconscious character cannot receive the benefits of a normal meal through this ability.

d10 · Pirate

The Cook gains greater control over preparation results and can feed more companions in less time.

Battle Cook

Passive

A dish's category Bonus is triggered by an ordinary Success, without requiring a Critical result.

The other effects of the result follow the Cooking rules as normal.

Speciality

Passive

Choose a dish from the Catalogue as your speciality.

When you prepare that dish, its Threshold is reduced by 2.

This benefit applies to the chosen dish and does not automatically extend to every recipe in the same category.

Cook’s Hand · Improved

Prerequisite: Cook’s Hand · Basic

One of your dishes can feed two companions instead of one.

The preparation result applies to both, who receive the dish's specified effects.

This version increases the number of recipients: it does not add another recovery die beyond the one already granted by the Basic version.

Express Cooking

Accelerated preparation

You can prepare a dish during a short break without necessarily using an entire work block.

The Talent reduces the time needed, but does not remove the recipe's ingredient requirements.

d12 · Veteran

The Cook becomes able to support the entire crew, intervene at critical moments and prepare meals even away from the ship's kitchen.

Cook’s Hand · Mastery

Prerequisite: Cook’s Hand · Improved

Your dishes also remove one removable negative Status from whoever consumes them.

A meal does not automatically remove a physical, environmental or chemical cause that continues to apply the Status.

Second Fire

  • 1×/combat
  • Reaction · Target within 5 m

When a companion within 5 metres falls below half their HP, you can use your Reaction to throw them a ration.

The companion immediately recovers:

1d6 ST

Second Fire does not restore HP and cannot be used more than once in the same combat.

War Banquet

Preparation before combat

Before a combat you have had the opportunity to prepare for, you can cook for the entire crew.

The preparation result applies to every companion who sits down to eat.

The Talent extends the result to the whole crew without requiring a separate preparation for each member.

The Counter

Passive

A fire and a work surface are enough for you to set up a field kitchen.

You can prepare dishes outside the ship's kitchen too, without penalties for lacking the usual equipment.

The required ingredients remain necessary.

Stolen Recipe

Passive

You only need to taste a dish once to learn how to reproduce it.

The recipe enters your repertoire and you can prepare it again using the necessary ingredients.

d20 · Legend

Supreme Talent · The Legendary Banquet

The Legendary Banquet

  • Ultimate
  • Once per adventure

The Cook prepares a banquet destined to become part of the crew's history.

Using it requires hours of preparation, real ingredients and a crew that sits down at the table.

No Cooking roll is required: the result is automatically treated as Critical for every companion who takes part.

Banquet effects

All participants receive the dish's effects as though it had been prepared with a Critical result.

HP and Stamina recovery is doubled.

Furthermore, until the end of the adventure, every participant receives:

+2 ST

Maximum Stamina

1 HP

Surviving the first fatal blow

The first blow that would reduce a participant to 0 HP instead leaves them at 1 HP.

This protection applies once to each character who ate at the banquet.

Requirements: the Cook's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

The Legendary Banquet cannot be activated instantly during combat. It requires actual preparation and affects only the characters who take part in the meal.

Progression · Cook’s Hand

Cook’s Hand is the Role's step-based progression path.

VersionGradeEffect
Basicd8+1 die to the resource recovered by the dish.
Improvedd10One dish can feed 2 companions.
Masteryd12The dish also removes one removable negative Status.

Each version requires the previous one. Unlike Talents that replace an ability with a higher version, Cook’s Hand progressively expands the dish's benefits.

Example · The meal before boarding

A Cook has Cooking d12 and has acquired Cook’s Hand · Mastery and War Banquet.

Before attacking a Navy ship, they prepare a dish for the crew.

Thanks to War Banquet, the preparation result applies to all companions who eat. Cook’s Hand increases any recovery provided by the dish by one die and also allows one removable negative Status to be removed from its recipients.

If the Cook also has Battle Cook, the category Bonus is triggered by an ordinary Success.

4.8 · Navigator

The Navigator turns a dangerous voyage into a navigable route. They study the weather, read currents, interpret nautical charts and guide the ship through seas that would challenge any other crew.

Their specialisation is not limited to travel. During a naval battle, an experienced Navigator can anticipate cannon fire, exploit weather conditions and bring the ship into positions the enemy did not expect.

Role Sheet

CharacteristicValue
RoleNavigator
Role SkillNavigation
Starting Graded8
PathSingle
TraitThe Road Across the Sea
SignaturePerfect Course

Navigation determines access to the Role's Talents and contributes to crossing Checks and the manoeuvres specified by their abilities.

Trait · The Road Across the Sea

Always active · Free

The Navigator specialises in sea crossings. Their expertise allows them to contribute directly to the rolls that determine the voyage's progress, turning knowledge of the sea into additional dice.

The specific ways in which they add Navigation to Checks are defined by the Talents and crossing rules.

Signature · Perfect Course

Always active · Free

When you achieve a margin of at least +4 on a Navigation Check related to a crossing, you find a particularly favourable route.

You gain both of the following benefits:

Prepared course. Until the destination is reached, the crew keeps 1d6 of Course. Before making a Check directly related to the same crossing, the Navigator can consume this die and add it to the Check.

For example, this could involve:

  • Overcoming a current.
  • Facing a stretch of storm.
  • Avoiding a shoal.
  • Staying on course during an unexpected event.
  • Making another crossing Check required by the voyage.

The die must be declared before the roll.

Only one Perfect Course can be kept at a time. Gaining the Signature again before using the die replaces the previous one.

Anticipated danger. The Game Master also reveals a real danger further along the route, if there is one that the course could reasonably allow you to foresee.

If the entire crossing is resolved with a single Check and no further Checks are required before arrival, the Course Die simply goes unused. The Signature's informational benefit still applies.

Talent Tree

Navigation GradeTier
d8Deckhand
d10Pirate
d12Veteran
d20Legend

Talents become available on reaching the required Grade, but must be acquired through Upgrades, observing any prerequisites.

d8 · Deckhand

These Talents introduce navigation's fundamental benefits: steering the ship, interpreting the weather, avoiding attacks and handling dangerous shallows.

Hand on the Helm

Passive

When you are at the helm, add your Navigation Die to the crossing roll.

The combination becomes:

Sails + Cunning + Navigation

The Talent applies when you personally steer the ship.

Eye of the Sky

Passive

Make Checks to forecast weather conditions with Advantage.

When you successfully predict an atmospheric hazard before entering it, the Game Master must tell you at least:

  • The general nature of the phenomenon.
  • The direction or area it comes from.
  • One concrete consequence it could cause.

The crew can then prepare before facing it.

Depending on the situation, appropriate preparation can:

  • Avoid a penalty that would have resulted from being caught by surprise.
  • Allow the cargo or sails to be secured.
  • Change the course.
  • Set up an appropriate Manoeuvre.

Eye of the Sky does not prevent the storm or automatically cancel all its consequences.

Born Helmsman · Basic

Passive

During a naval battle, the ship adds your Navigation Die to its evasive manoeuvres.

This benefit represents your ability to read the trajectories of cannon fire and adjust the course to avoid it.

Sound the Depths

Passive

You know the seabed beneath the keel and can guide the ship through shoals, narrow channels and shallow water.

You can exploit these passages to escape pursuit by larger ships, which risk running aground if they try to follow you.

The Talent allows you to use the seabed's features as an advantage while navigating.

d10 · Pirate

The Navigator gains advanced planning and cartography abilities. During naval battles they can avoid a particularly dangerous hit and gain greater freedom to manoeuvre.

Master Course

Passive

When you plot a course, you always anticipate one danger along it, even without achieving a Critical result.

The benefit therefore does not require the Signature's +4 margin.

Cartographer

Passive

The nautical charts you create are considered detailed charts.

They grant:

+d8

to Checks that provide a benefit for a detailed nautical chart.

Your charts are reusable and have high commercial value in ports.

Born Helmsman · Improved

  • 1× per naval battle
  • Prerequisite: Born Helmsman · Basic

You can completely negate one incoming cannon shot.

The ship avoids the hit through a manoeuvre executed at the right moment.

The Talent negates a single cannon shot, not the enemy's entire salvo.

Fair Winds

Passive

While you are at the helm and the ship has usable sails, the ship can perform +1 naval Manoeuvre per round.

Fair Winds increases the ship's Manoeuvre limit, but does not grant characters additional Actions.

The second Manoeuvre must therefore be performed by a crew member who still has their Action and occupies the appropriate station.

d12 · Veteran

The Navigator can face extreme conditions, guide the crew through apparently impossible routes and exploit the sea as a tactical element.

Born Helmsman · Mastery

Prerequisite: Born Helmsman · Improved

During a naval battle, the ship can manoeuvre and fire in the same turn without penalty.

The Talent removes the penalty associated with this combination, without automatically granting additional cannon shots or Actions.

Being able to manoeuvre and fire in the same round permits both naval Manoeuvres. The helm Manoeuvre and Cannon Shot must still be carried out from their respective stations and use the Actions of the characters involved.

Impossible Course

Passive

You can guide the ship through routes that other crews would normally have to sail around.

You can tackle whirlpools, fog banks, magnetic zones, opposing currents and shoals, using your knowledge to identify a navigable route.

The Talent allows you to overcome particularly complex navigation obstacles, but does not make the ship indestructible.

Harness the Storm

Passive

When you are at the helm, you and the ship ignore penalties to Navigation rolls and naval Manoeuvres caused exclusively by:

  • Rain.
  • Natural fog.
  • Rough seas.
  • Strong wind.
  • Storms.

The phenomenon's physical effects still exist.

A storm can still damage a sail, a wave can still sweep the deck and fog can still physically obscure something that the available instruments cannot locate.

Enemy ships without an equivalent ability continue to suffer the normal penalties.

If you deliberately lead them into weather conditions you have mastered, they make Checks directly hindered by that phenomenon with Disadvantage.

Never Lost

Passive

You always know where you are, where you came from and the direction of the open sea.

This ability also works in darkness and below deck.

You can also find your way back to any place you have visited at least once.

The Talent concerns your sense of direction and memory of routes: it does not automatically reveal the location of unknown places.

Escape Route

Passive

When you enter an unfamiliar place, you identify a way out.

You and the characters following you can take it at full speed without getting lost or making rolls to find your way.

The Talent does not automatically remove any enemies or physical obstacles along the way.

d20 · Legend

Supreme Talent · The Course No One Knows

The Course No One Knows

  • Ultimate
  • Once per adventure

The Navigator can identify a route that no one else would have recognised.

The Talent can be used in two different situations.

At sea · Impossible crossing

Declare a destination that:

  • You know or can identify through a chart, a Log Pose, an Eternal Pose or another reliable indication.
  • Is physically reachable by sea using the ship in question.

You identify an extraordinary route and the ship completes the crossing without making the usual Navigation Checks.

During the voyage:

  • There are no random encounters or unwanted deviations.
  • Storms, currents, fog and ordinary navigation hazards do not prevent you from reaching the destination.
  • The voyage still takes the route's usual approximate travel time.
  • The ship continues to consume provisions and other resources as normal.
  • The Ultimate does not allow you to cross dry land, impassable walls, seas physically inaccessible to the ship or other obstacles that would make the voyage itself impossible.

Unavoidable narrative events directly related to the destination are not cancelled by the ability.

In naval battle · Legendary manoeuvre

The ship leaves the enemy's line of fire and reappears in an unexpected position.

The enemy loses their turn and your ship fires first.

Requirements: the Navigator's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

The Course No One Knows represents the pinnacle of the Navigator's specialisation: outside combat it allows an extraordinary crossing to be completed; during a naval battle it directly changes the ships' positions and the order of events.

4.9 · Engineer

A pirate ship survives through more than its crew's strength. The hull must withstand cannon fire, engines must keep running and someone must be able to build and maintain what the crew needs.

The Engineer handles the crew's construction, maintenance and technology. The player chooses one of two Styles, each devoted to a different specialisation.

Role Sheet

CharacteristicValue
RoleEngineer
Available StylesCarpenter, Mechanic
Starting Role Skill Graded8

The Role Skill depends on the chosen Style.

StyleSkillSpecialisation
CarpenterCarpentryHull, structures and naval repairs
MechanicMechanicsMachinery, cannons and mechanical components

Each Style's Talents become available at Grades d8, d10, d12 and d20 of the associated Skill. They must still be acquired through Upgrades.

4.9.1 · Carpenter

The Carpenter knows every plank of the ship, from the keel to the mainmast. They repair damage, reinforce structures and make the hull stronger than it was when built.

Their Role Skill is Carpentry d8.

Trait · Timber and Hull

Free · Always active

The Carpenter specialises in building and repairing the hull. During a naval battle, their station is dedicated to repairs, while outside combat they can handle the ship's structural development.

Signature · A Repair That Holds

Free · Always active

When you achieve a margin of at least +4 on an Emergency Repair Check, double the SP recovered by that repair.

First calculate ordinary recovery, including any additional dice granted by Talents, then double the total.

The Signature only modifies recovered SP and does not double the repair's other effects.

d8 Talents · Deckhand

  • Emergency Repair
  • Action · Naval battle

You can use the Emergency Repair manoeuvre from Chapter 13.

A successful Check restores 1 Carpentry Die of SP to the ship, before applying any Talents or the Signature.

  • Lumberjack
  • Passive

When searching for timber or building materials, add your Carpentry Die to the Gathering roll.

  • Hands on Wood · Basic
  • Passive

Your Emergency Repairs recover +1 Carpentry Die of SP.

You therefore normally recover 2 Carpentry Dice instead of one.

  • Barricade
  • Action · Within 5 metres

Using materials already present and immediately available — planks, crates, sheet metal, furniture, wreckage or similar items — quickly assemble shelter within 5 metres.

Barricade does not create materials from nothing or allow you to build a complex structure in a few seconds.

The shelter can occupy up to approximately 3 metres in width and normally protect up to three human-sized characters positioned behind it.

It grants Partial Cover, and therefore Passive Defence +2.

If the physical arrangement completely covers a character, the GM can apply the normal Full Cover rules.

The Barricade can be attacked and destroyed like an ordinary object or scenery element.

If the available materials cannot realistically be assembled into shelter, the Talent cannot be used.

d10 Talents · Pirate

  • Travelling Shipyard
  • Passive

You can repair and upgrade the ship even on the open sea, without having to reach a port.

The time needed is halved.

  • Module Master
  • Passive

Through structural adaptation, a ship on which you have completed the work gains +1 Module slot.

This is a permanent improvement to the ship.

Multiple Carpenters cannot apply the same benefit to the same ship more than once.

The additional slot does not automatically grant a Module.

  • Hands on Wood · Improved
  • Prerequisite: Hands on Wood · Basic

With the same Action, you can repair the hull and restore a damaged Module to working order.

The Module must be damaged, not destroyed.

  • Boarding Bridge
  • Action

When the two ships are in the Alongside band, you can quickly set up gangways, ropes and grappling hooks already available on board to create a connection.

The Talent represents the rapid deployment of prepared equipment: it does not build a gangway from raw timber on the spot.

To create a passage that can actually be walked across, the physical distance between the two decks cannot normally exceed 10 metres.

The crew can cross the connection without making ordinary boarding rolls and without provoking Reaction attacks caused by crossing.

The route can be used in either direction.

If the ships move beyond the distance the connection can sustain, the links break or are released and Boarding Bridge ends.

An enemy can also physically destroy or cut gangways, ropes and grappling hooks.

d12 Talents · Veteran

  • Hands on Wood · Mastery
  • Once per naval battle
  • Prerequisite: Hands on Wood · Improved

You can restore a destroyed Module to working order, not merely a damaged one.

  • Reinforced Hull
  • Passive

After completing reinforcement work on the hull, the Damage Dice of cannon shots hitting the ship decrease by one step.

Follow the normal scale:

d20 → d12
d12 → d10
d10 → d8
d8 → d6
d6 → d4

This Talent cannot reduce a d4 any further.

If a cannon shot uses multiple dice of the same Grade, all its Damage Dice are reduced by one step.

Reinforced Hull modifies cannon shot damage, not the attack roll.

The benefit belongs to the reinforced ship and does not require the Carpenter's presence.

  • Breaking Point
  • Passive

You recognise the weak points of structures and objects. Your strikes against these targets deal full damage without requiring a roll.

During a naval battle you can also target a specific Module on the enemy ship.

  • Fortification
  • Preparation

With at least a few hours of work and appropriate materials, you can turn an existing position into a defensible point.

The Talent normally applies to:

  • A room.
  • An entrance.
  • A section of deck.
  • A small encampment.
  • Another equivalent, limited area.

Those defending the position benefit from Cover.

The approaches you have prepared can also become Difficult Terrain for anyone trying to reach it.

Fortification uses structures and materials that are actually available.

It does not allow you to build a fortress, a monumental wall, a gigantic bridge or other works requiring an actual Shipyard project in a few hours.

  • Grown Ship
  • Once per ship
  • Prerequisite: Module Master

At the end of a complete structural refit, choose: Hull · Sails · Artillery · Structure.

The chosen value permanently increases by one step, following the normal naval scale: d4 → d6 → d8 → d10 → d12.

Grown Ship cannot raise a statistic beyond d12.

The benefit belongs to the ship and does not require your presence once the work is complete.

The same ship can receive Grown Ship only once, regardless of the number of Carpenters present.

Supreme Talent d20 · She Will Not Sink

She Will Not Sink

  • Ultimate
  • Once per naval battle

When the ship would drop to 0 SP or less, it instead remains at 1 SP.

From that moment until the end of the battle:

  • The ship cannot fall below 1 SP.
  • It can continue performing any manoeuvres the situation makes possible as normal.
  • All its Destroyed Modules temporarily function as though operational.

Subsequent hits still strike the ship and may have narrative consequences, damage components or affect characters on board, but they do not reduce the hull below 1 SP while the Ultimate remains active.

She Will Not Sink does not actually repair the damage.

At the end of the battle:

  • The ship remains at 1 SP.
  • The Modules return to their actual previous condition.
  • The Ultimate's benefit ends.
  • The ship needs a real Shipyard for structural repairs.

There is no automatic countdown after the battle: the Ultimate does not make the ship sink after a fixed number of minutes or hours.

The ship nevertheless remains in Critical condition. Storms, further battles, leaks still open or other hazards can naturally put it at risk again.

Requirements: the Engineer's Ambition and the Game Master's permission.

4.9.2 · Mechanic

The Mechanic keeps engines, cannons, gears and devices operational. Where the Carpenter handles the hull, the Mechanic ensures that the machines work.

They can also repair a Cyborg's artificial components.

Their Role Skill is Mechanics d8.

Trait · Living Machines

Free · Always active

The Mechanic specialises in maintaining and operating devices, cannons and engines.

Operating machinery

A machine does not act independently unless its sheet says so.

Using it normally requires an operator and the Action, manoeuvre or station specified by the device.

When an ability allows a machine to act twice, the second activation:

  • Does not require a second Action from the operator.
  • Is resolved separately.
  • Consumes the required ammunition, fuel, energy or other resources again.
  • Makes any rolls required by the machine again.
