A Custom Technique can be created at any Grade permitted by the Attribute used.

It is therefore not obliged to start at d4.

For example, a character with Technique d8 can create their own Technique based on Technique directly at d8, or voluntarily choose to create it at d4 or d6.

Any limits imposed by the Source, Style, Fruit or other abilities involved still apply.

During the campaign, the character can create further Techniques without a universal numerical limit, provided they meet the necessary requirements and the Game Master approves their identity and compatibility.

A new Technique created during the campaign also cannot exceed the Grade of the Attribute it uses.

7.4 · Sources

The Source determines the Technique's origin. The selectable Sources for built Techniques are Style and Fruit.

Source · Style

A Style Technique comes from the character's training and uses only the effects assigned to their Style (§7.8). The Technique's Grade does not automatically expand the available catalogue. Striker fights unarmed; Swordsman requires a compatible blade; Sniper uses a compatible ranged weapon; Crusher requires a compatible blunt weapon.

Effects granted by ammunition, poisons, preparations, bombs, Modules and other objects belong to the profile of the object actually used. The Builder does not create them from nothing or replace the doses, Thresholds, durations or usage methods of consumable items.

Source · Fruit

A Fruit Technique represents an application of the character's power. It can access further effects, Areas, Ranges or Persistent Zones only when the Power’s Core and Fruit Sheet make that result genuinely possible and authorise its use. The GM checks compatibility with the Source.

A Zoan transformation alone does not grant an Area or Zone. Any special powers, for example those of a Mythical Zoan, follow the abilities explicitly defined on the Sheet. The Fruit does not ignore Grade, slots or ST costs except where specific rules apply.

Weapons and special devices

A special weapon or Module can explicitly grant further effects or builds when its rule and function make them possible. Material capability alone does not expand the catalogue. The Technique's Grade, slots and costs are still required unless an exception is stated.

Racial and Haki Techniques

The racial Technique and Haki abilities remain sources of the character's power, but they are not created by freely choosing effects through the ordinary Builder's two Sources. Their rules depend on the Race and Haki Colour possessed.

7.5 · Forms

The Form determines the Technique's main function.

FormFunction
SingleA Technique directed at one target.
AreaA Technique affecting a portion of space, if authorised by the Source.
DisplacementA Technique devoted to mobility.
DefenceA Technique for protecting yourself.
EnhancementA Technique that temporarily improves the character's abilities.
SongA Technique exclusive to the Musician.

The Defence, Displacement and Song Forms use only their respective effect families. Launch, Dash and Leap can also be included in other compatible Forms, but only for Styles that possess them (§7.8.3).

Area

The Area Form is not a universal Style option. Swordsman and Crusher can use only Close Area. Striker and Sniper have no ordinary Style Area Form.

Close Area requires minimum Grade d8 and costs 2 ST. It is a circle centred on the character, with radius equal to the actual Reach of the weapon used. It has no radius of its own and cannot receive Range. The template takes no Effect slots; its 2 ST are added to the costs of the Technique's other effects. A weapon with Reach 5 m therefore generates an Area of radius 5 m. Damage and Statuses follow the normal Area rules (§7.9.3).

Cones, lines, extended circles and distant Areas require a special Source authorising them, such as a Fruit, special weapon or another explicit ability. Area bombs, ammunition and preparations follow their own profiles.

Enhancement

The Enhancement Form uses a main Action to apply its effects. It must not be confused with activating a Talent or Haki through a Bonus Action.

Defensive use of attacking Techniques

Using a compatible attacking Technique as Active Defence does not require the Defence Form or a Counterattack effect. It follows §6.10, using a Reaction, immediate costs and the Technique's normal roll.

7.6 · Effect slots

Slots determine how many effects a Technique can contain.

d4 and d6 Techniques have 0 Effect slots; d8 and d10 have 2, d12 has 3 and d20 has 4. Further abilities may provide additional slots or explicit exceptions.

Technique GradeEffect Slots
d40
d60
d82
d102
d123
d204

A Technique can contain fewer effects than the maximum available. Filling every slot is not compulsory.

Unlocking effects

Selecting an effect requires its minimum Grade, an available slot and Style, Source and Form compatibility simultaneously. An effect's d6 requirement does not grant the Technique a slot.

In the ordinary catalogue, effects with a d6 requirement therefore normally become usable from d8 too, when the Technique has slots. An ability explicitly granting additional slots or making an effect slot-free can provide an exception.

Family limit

Normally, a Technique can contain only one effect per family. Enhancement effects follow the Builder's specific rules.

An Area Technique uses a single template. The template takes no slots, but retains its minimum Grade, cost and Source authorisation. A special Area can have at most one Range, which takes one slot unless explicit exceptions apply. Close Area cannot have Range.

7.7 · Stamina costs

A Technique used without enhancements does not automatically require Stamina.

Additional effects have specific costs, expressed in ST.

When multiple effects are selected, their costs are added together according to the Builder's summary.

Some effects cost 0 ST and add no cost of their own. The total cost is the result of the Builder's summary, applying the calculation rules and any specified discounts: do not manually assign 1 ST to an effect listed at zero cost.

A Special Move does not automatically reduce these costs. Any discounts must come from Talents, equipment or other effects that explicitly provide them.

When costs are paid

Every cost required to use a Technique, Talent, ability, consumable, ammunition, dose or charge is paid when its use is declared and before its outcome is known.

A missed attack, failed Check, successful opponent Defence or subsequent interruption does not return resources already spent.

There are no automatic refunds.

A resource is returned only when an ability explicitly says so.

7.8 · Effect catalogue

The Builder defines what a Technique does; Talents define how well the character fights in their Style. The ordinary catalogue is not universal: each Style uses only the effects listed on its own sheet.

The Technique Die already determines base damage. There are no longer ordinary effects that directly purchase +1d6, +1d8 or +1d10 Damage Die. Talents, Signatures, Haki, Fruits, weapons, Modules and ammunition may grant additional power under their own rules. The specific conditions of Charge, All or Nothing and Crash remain.

Grades indicate the minimum requirement; they do not grant slots or effects. With 0 slots at d4/d6, effects taking slots are normally usable from d8 (§7.6).

7.8.1 · Special damage

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Charged102 STCrusherSkip a turn; if you are not hit, on the following turn you double the Damage Dice, attack with Advantage and ignore Cover.
All or Nothingd125 STSwordsmanIf you miss, you cannot defend against the next attack; on a Clean Hit, damage is tripled.

Charge is not Running

Charge is offensive preparation: the character forgoes the specified turn and must avoid being hit to gain the benefits on the following turn. It does not introduce a Run Action or freely double Movement.

7.8.2 · Control

When a Technique effect applies a Status granting a Save, the Threshold is determined by the Technique's Grade (§1.4). This also applies to the special Blinding, Terror, Paralysis and Slumber profiles when authorised by the Source. Grapple retains the Strength contest even when applying Immobilised; Laceration and Bleeding do not grant an automatic Save. Substances retain their own specific Threshold.

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Unbalanced61 STStriker, CrusherApplies Off-Balance under the Status's rules.
Lacerationd61 STSwordsman, SniperApplies Bleeding; the same Status does not stack with itself.
Grappled81 ST/turnStrikerImmobilises while physically sustained; Strength contest each turn.
Breakthroughd82 STStriker, CrusherApplies Stunned for 1 turn; grants a Save under the Status's rules.

Grapple

When the Technique applies Grapple, spend 1 ST. The target remains Immobilised while you physically maintain the hold and keep paying its cost. At the start of each subsequent turn, before acting, you can spend 1 ST to sustain it; if you do not, it ends immediately. The effect's normal Strength contest is also resolved each turn. The Technique must actually allow you to grasp and hold the target.

7.8.3 · Displacement

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Launchd61 STStriker, CrusherPushes the target 3 m.
Dashd61 STStriker, Swordsman, SniperDisplace up to half your Movement before or after the hit.
Leapd61 STStriker, SwordsmanReach a distant or elevated position up to half your Movement away.

Launch, Dash and Leap can also be included in a Technique of another compatible Form, only by the listed Styles. These effects are not available to all Styles. Each effect takes its own slot, pays its own cost and retains the conditions needed to perform it.

Dash and Leap depend on the character's Movement: with Movement 10 m, the ordinary distance is up to 5 m. Pursue, Yank and Swap have been removed. Attraction requires a Source, object or ability that actually grasps and drags the target.

7.8.4 · Defence

Ordinary Defence effects are available for the Defence Form.

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Guardd8–d201–4 STStriker, Swordsman, CrusherIncreases PD until the start of the next turn according to the chosen version.
Ally Shieldd81 STCrusherTransfers the hit directed at an ally within 5 m onto you, observing the normal Defence conditions.

Guard · Grade progression

Minimum GradeVersionCostPD Bonus
d8Basic Guard1 ST+2
d10Improved Guard2 ST+3
d12Mastery Guard3 ST+4
d20Supreme Guard4 ST+5

The chosen version requires at least the listed Technique Grade, within its Attribute's limit. Each higher version replaces the previous one: bonuses and costs do not add together. Guard takes one slot and lasts until the start of your next turn.

Counterattack and Parry are no longer Builder effects. A compatible attacking Technique can be used directly as Active Defence (§6.10); a compatible weapon permits an ordinary Parry. Barrier requires a Source or ability explicitly generating that protection.

7.8.5 · Enhancement

Enhancement Techniques require a main Action. Their effects last for the scene and do not use the Bonus Action reserved for active Talents and Haki.

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Furyd83 STStriker, CrusherFor the scene: +1 step to compatible Techniques' Damage Die; PD −1.
Aimd83 STSwordsman, SniperFor the scene: Advantage on attack rolls with compatible Techniques.

Fury increases only the Damage Die of compatible Techniques, within the normal d20 limit: it does not increase the Technique Die for hitting, the Technique's Grade or its slots. Aim grants Advantage on the attack roll and does not increase the die.

Momentum, Toughness and Beyond the Limit have been removed from the ordinary Builder. Attributes and Skills with their own Prestige progression follow their rules; a temporary increase does not automatically grant new Talents or Prestige benefits.

7.8.6 · Area

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Close Aread82 STSwordsman, CrusherCircle centred on yourself; radius equal to the weapon's actual Reach. No Range; template takes no slots.

Close Area requires minimum Grade d8 and costs 2 ST. It is a circle centred on the character, with radius equal to the actual Reach of the weapon used. It has no radius of its own and cannot receive Range. The template takes no Effect slots; its 2 ST are added to the costs of the Technique's other effects. A weapon with Reach 5 m therefore generates an Area of radius 5 m. Damage and Statuses follow the normal Area rules (§7.9.3).

7.8.7 · Chained

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Crashd82 STStriker, CrusherIf the target collides with a surface, obstacle or creature, they take +1d8 damage.
Ricochetd102 STSniperSecond target within 5 m of the first; half damage.
Chaind122 STSwordsman, SniperUp to three targets in a line: full damage, half, half of the second.

These effects take slots and follow the conditions of §7.11. Hook has been removed.

7.8.8 · Profiles for special Sources

Blinding, Terror, Paralysis, Slumber, Attraction and Barrier are reference profiles: they do not belong to the ordinary catalogue of any Style. They can be selected only if a Source or ability explicitly grants them. Consumable objects instead apply their own profiles.

Special profileMin. GradeCostHow it works
Blindingd81 STApplies Blinded; grants a Save.
Terrord81 STApplies Terrified; grants a Save under the Status's rules.
Paralysisd122 STApplies Paralysed for 1 turn; grants a Save.
Slumberd122 STApplies Asleep; grants a Save.
Attractiond81 STDrags the target 3 m towards you, if the Source can grasp them.
Barrierd81 STGrants Cover to you and allies within 5 m, if the Source generates the protection.

Poison uses a dose with its own effects, Threshold, duration and usage method. Heavy Strike, Overload and Annihilating Blow have been removed only as ordinary effects: independent Talents and abilities with the same names keep their own rules.

7.9 · Area Techniques

The Area Form affects a portion of space. Access depends on the Style or a special Source and is not universal.

Style Close Area

Swordsman and Crusher can build only Close Area around their own position. Striker and Sniper have no ordinary Style Area Form.

Close Area requires minimum Grade d8 and costs 2 ST. It is a circle centred on the character, with radius equal to the actual Reach of the weapon used. It has no radius of its own and cannot receive Range. The template takes no Effect slots; its 2 ST are added to the costs of the Technique's other effects. A weapon with Reach 5 m therefore generates an Area of radius 5 m. Damage and Statuses follow the normal Area rules (§7.9.3).

Sovereign Slash does not project Close Area at a distance. Titanic Wave, Seismic Wave and other Prestige propagations retain their own templates, conditions and costs, distinct from the Area Form.

Special Areas

Cones, lines, extended circles, Ranges and other Area profiles are available only when a special Source makes them possible and authorises their construction. A Fruit or special weapon can grant the following templates according to its description, observing Grade, slots and costs. Bombs, ammunition, preparations and other usable items apply their own profiles directly.

7.9.1 · Special Area templates

EffectMin. GradeSTArea
Breathd605 m cone
Waved8110 m cone
Tided12320 m cone
Furrowd6010 m line, approximately 1 m wide
Lanced10220 m line
Riftd12440 m line
Burstd81Circle of radius 3 m
Explosiond102Circle of radius 5 m
Detonationd124Circle of radius 10 m
Cataclysmd206Circle of radius 20 m

A Technique contains only one template, which takes no slots but retains its own cost and Grade requirement. These templates do not replace Close Area in the ordinary catalogue.

7.9.2 · Special Area Range

Range determines how far away the origin of a special Area authorised by the Source can be placed.

EffectMin. GradeSTDistance
Short Ranged61Up to 10 m
Medium Ranged82Up to 20 m
Long Ranged103Up to 40 m

Range is optional and takes one Effect slot. Without Range, the special Area starts from the character and can affect them directly. Close Area cannot receive Range.

Only a special Area with Range can be used beyond its distance band with the Threshold increased by 4, if compatible with the Source. This rule does not project Close Area or grant greater range to Sniper weapons: that comes from the weapon and abilities extending it.

7.9.3 · Area damage and Statuses

Targets affected by the Area Technique take half damage. For Statuses applied through the Area that grant a Save, the Threshold is determined by the Grade of the Technique generating the Area. Bleeding, substances and Grapples retain their specific procedures. These rules also apply to Close Area unless a specific ability explicitly provides an exception.

7.9.4 · Eye of the Storm

Special profile: d12, 1 ST, one Effect slot unless explicit exceptions apply. Allows you to choose which targets are not hit by an already authorised Area Technique. It does not itself grant access to the Area Form or expand the Styles' ordinary catalogue. The None of My Own Talent modifies slots, cost and requirements for Fruit Techniques under its own rules.

7.10 · Persistent Zones

Some Techniques do not end at the moment of impact. A poisonous cloud can remain suspended over the deck, a fire can keep burning or a mass of mud can turn the ground into a trap.

These effects are called Persistent Zones.

A Persistent Zone is built through a Technique with the Area Form. It uses the template chosen for that Technique and inherits its dimensions.

A Zone cannot be assigned a size independent of the Area generating it.

7.10.1 · Source requirements

Persistent Zones are special Source effects and do not belong to the Styles' ordinary catalogue. To create one through a Technique, the Source must be able to actually leave matter, energy or a persistent condition in the environment and explicitly authorise this use.

The GM checks compatibility with the power or the design of the special weapon or device. Merely having an Area does not authorise a Zone; Grade, slots and costs remain required.

SourceOptions
LogiaIf the element and Sheet authorise a persistent Zone.
ParameciaIf the power and Sheet authorise a persistent condition.
ZoanTransformation alone does not grant Zones; any special powers require authorisation on the Sheet.
Special weapon or deviceIf its rule and design explicitly grant a Zone. Consumables use their own profiles.

A smoke Logia could create a Curtain; an adhesive Paramecia could generate a Prison, if its Sheet authorises these applications. Bombs, smoke bombs, preparations and poisons creating a zone apply their own profiles directly and are not rebuilt with the Builder.

The mechanical effect must be compatible with what the Technique produces in the story.

7.10.2 · Persistent Zone catalogue

Altered Ground, Quagmire, Curtain and Prison remain reference profiles for special Sources, not options a Style can freely purchase.

EffectMinimum GradeCostHow it works
Altered Groundd82 STDeals 1d6 damage to anyone in the Zone at the start of their turn.
Altered Ground Lv2d103 STDeals 2d6 damage at the start of the turn.
Altered Ground Lv3d124 STDeals 3d6 damage at the start of the turn.
Quagmired81 STCreates Difficult Terrain: Movement halved and Charge cannot be performed.
Curtaind81 STBlocks sight. Anyone inside trying to aim outside is treated as Blinded.
Prisond122 STRestrains anyone in the Zone. Leaving requires a successful Save.

Altered Ground

Altered Ground deals damage at the start of the turns of creatures in the Zone.

The damage depends on the selected effect level.

The three levels are alternatives within the same family: they cannot be freely added together to accumulate damage.

Example: a Technique creates a circle of flames with Altered Ground Lv2. An enemy in the Zone at the start of their turn takes 2d6 damage.

Quagmire

Quagmire makes crossing the Zone difficult.

Movement is halved and Charge cannot be performed within it.

The terrain's appearance depends on the Source: it could be quicksand, uneven ice, debris or a substance produced by a Fruit.

Curtain

Curtain blocks visibility.

Anyone inside it is treated as Blinded when trying to aim outside.

The Curtain is not automatically a physical barrier: blocking sight does not mean blocking passage.

Prison

Prison restrains targets present in the Zone.

A trapped character must succeed on a Save using the Attribute consistent with the prison's form against the Threshold determined by the Technique's Grade (§1.4). A specific sheet may list a different Attribute, Threshold or procedure.

The prison's actual form depends on the Technique: roots, stone, ice, adhesive matter or other compatible elements.

7.11 · Chained Effects

The ordinary Chained Effects are Crash, Ricochet and Chain. Hook has been removed. The catalogue remains limited to the listed Styles.

EffectMin. GradeCostStyleHow it works
Crashd82 STStriker, CrusherIf the target collides with a surface, obstacle or creature, they take +1d8 damage.
Ricochetd102 STSniperSecond target within 5 m of the first; half damage.
Chaind122 STSwordsman, SniperUp to three targets in a line: full damage, half, half of the second.

7.11.1 · Crash

Crash (d8, 2 ST, Striker/Crusher) deals +1d8 damage if the target collides with a surface, obstacle or another creature. It suits a Technique that also has Launch: the additional die requires a valid collision and is not a generic damage purchase.

Combination · Launch + Crash

Harbour Impact

Crusher Technique d8 with Launch (1 ST) + Crash (2 ST): total effect cost 3 ST and 2 slots occupied.

Make the normal attack roll. If the attack hits, it deals the normal Damage Die and pushes the target 3 m. If Launch causes a valid collision, it adds 1d8 damage.

Launch belongs to the Displacement family, Crash to Chained Effects. The combination observes the ordinary limit of one effect per family and the d8 Technique's 2 slots.

7.11.2 · Ricochet

Ricochet (d10, 2 ST, Sniper) allows the shot to reach a second target within 5 m of the first, dealing half damage. It grants no further targets; normal attack and defence conditions still apply.

7.11.3 · Chain

Chain (d12, 2 ST, Swordsman/Sniper) reaches up to three targets in a line. Make separate attack rolls; damage is rolled only once.

TargetDamage taken
FirstFull damage
SecondHalf damage
ThirdHalf the second target's damage

Example: with 16 damage, the targets take 16, 8 and 4 respectively, provided their respective attacks hit. Chain does not automatically extend the weapon's or Source's reach.

7.12 · Musician Melodies

The Musician has an exclusive category of Techniques: Songs.

A Song is built by choosing the Song Form and a Melody from its Catalogue.

Each Technique of this type can contain only one Melody.

Songs do not use the normal attack roll.

Songs rule

Activation: the Musician spends their Action and the Stamina listed by the Melody.

Allies: automatically receive the effect without making any rolls.

Enemies: make a Save when the Melody requires it.

A Song does not trigger the Musician's Signature through a +4 margin, since the Musician does not make a roll to perform it.

Song Thresholds

When a Song requires a Save, its Threshold is determined by the musical instrument's Grade actually used.

Instrument GradeThreshold
d43
d64
d85
d106
d127
d2011

The instrument's usable Grade cannot exceed Art.

Example: a Musician with Art d8 who owns a d12 instrument temporarily uses that instrument as d8; their Songs therefore have Threshold 5.

The voice is always available as a d4 instrument and therefore normally produces Threshold 3.

The Save uses the Attribute specified by the Melody or applied Status.

If the Melody specifies no Attribute but acts on the target's will, emotions or mind, normally use Spirit.

7.12.1 · Melody Catalogue

MelodyMinimum GradeCostEffect
Tune of Vigourd62 STAn ally who can hear you recovers 1d6 ST. Each character benefits only once per scene; in the same scene, the cost increases by +1 ST for each different target that has already benefited. See Tune of Vigour below.
Calld61 STEnemies within 20 metres who hear you must attack you or attack other targets with Disadvantage. Save.
War Marchd62 ST + 1 ST/turnSustained Song: one chosen ally gains +1d4 to attacks while you keep playing.
Countersongd81 STNegates Terror, Slumber or charm on allies.
Mocking Requiemd81 STOne enemy suffers Disadvantage on their next roll. Save.
Tempod82 STOne ally moves their Initiative immediately after yours for the rest of combat.
Crescendod102 STOne ally's next Technique costs 2 ST less, to a minimum of 1 ST.
Lullabyd104 STEnemies within 20 metres who hear you become Asleep if they fail their Save. Deals no damage.
Overwhelming Solod105 STListening allies recover 1d6 ST and gain +1d6 to their attack roll. +1 ST for every additional use in the same scene.
Crew Anthemd125 STFor the scene, each companion chooses one of their dice and rolls it twice, keeping the better result.
Shipboard Songd124 STFor the scene, every companion at a naval station adds +1 die to their station's roll.
Funeral Marchd123 ST + 1 ST/turnRequires the Funeral March Talent. Sustained Song: every enemy within 10 metres who hears you makes a Spirit Save; those who fail are Terrified while they remain within reach, hear you and you keep playing.
Song of the Sould206 STSupreme Talent · Once per scene: crew members who can hear you, including the Musician, restore Stamina to its maximum. The same crew benefits only once per scene.

Melodies apply only the listed effects. A Song restoring Stamina does not automatically restore Hit Points too.

Tune of Vigour

d6 · 2 ST

An ally who can hear you recovers 1d6 ST.

The same character can benefit from Tune of Vigour only once per scene, regardless of the number of Musicians present.

If you use the Tune on a new target during the same scene, the cost increases by +1 ST for each different target who already benefited through your previous performances.

Example:

  • First companion → 2 ST.
  • Second companion → 3 ST.
  • Third companion → 4 ST.

The Musician can choose themself as the target, but even then can benefit from the Tune only once per scene.

Recovery cannot exceed Maximum ST.

The Tune of Vigour limit does not apply to Overwhelming Solo: they are different Melodies. Any limits and cost increases for Overwhelming Solo remain those listed on its sheet.

Sustained Songs

Only a Melody explicitly labelled a Sustained Song, or whose text states it continues while the Musician plays, requires upkeep.

Performing the Song normally requires:

  • The Action.
  • The initial cost listed by the Melody.

Starting from the next turn, sustaining a Sustained Song normally requires:

  • 1 ST per turn.
  • The Musician's Action.

Paying upkeep is not a new activation and does not apply the Melody's initial effects again.

Counterpoint · Basic removes the requirement to use the Action to sustain the Song: the Musician can act as normal while continuing to play.

Counterpoint · Improved allows two Songs to be sustained at once.

Counterpoint · Mastery removes the ST upkeep cost.

A Sustained Song ends immediately if:

  • The Musician chooses to stop it.
  • They do not pay upkeep when required.
  • They lose the ability to produce the necessary music.
  • They lose the required instrument and cannot continue using that same means.
  • They become Unconscious.
  • A condition or effect physically prevents them from singing or playing.

Simply being moved, knocked to the ground or involved in combat does not automatically interrupt a Song.

Unwavering Music provides a specific exception: Stunned and being knocked to the ground do not, on their own, interrupt an ongoing Song.

If the instrument used is lost or destroyed, the Song ends. The Musician can subsequently perform a new Song using their voice or another available instrument.

Reach and hearing

Unless a Melody specifies a different reach, a Song has an ordinary reach of:

20 metres

Being within that distance is not enough: the recipient must also actually hear the music.

Line of sight is not required.

Thick walls, completely isolated spaces, supernatural silence or other obstacles genuinely preventing sound from reaching the target block the Song.

An open door, window or other opening may instead allow sound to spread as normal.

Underwater, a Song works only if the Musician can actually produce music and the target can perceive it through the means used.

A Den Den Mushi or another means of communication can transmit an ordinary performance, but does not automatically transmit a Song's mechanical effects unless an ability explicitly says otherwise.

7.12.2 · Musical instruments

The Musician's instruments have a Grade expressed by a die.

Unlike weapons, they are not normally used to hit. Their Grade determines the difficulty of resisting Songs.

DieInstrument Grade
d4Basic
d6Competent
d8Expert
d10Master
d12Legend
d20Mythic

The instrument's usable Grade cannot exceed the Musician's Art Die.

A character with Art d8 who obtains a d12 instrument cannot immediately exploit its full quality. To use the instrument fully, they must develop Art.

The voice is always an available option, with Basic Grade d4.

7.12.3 · Instrument types

Each instrument type offers a particular amplification.

TypeAmplification
StringsMelodies directed at a single target cost 1 ST less, minimum 1.
WindSongs' reach increases from 20 to 40 metres.
PercussionMelodies affecting the whole crew cost 1 ST less.
BellowsSustained Songs' upkeep is free for the first two turns.
VoiceYou cannot be disarmed and no one recognises the Technique's use before it produces its effect.

Instrument type is independent of Grade: a better instrument does not automatically change its amplification.

Unusual instruments may have an additional special effect, defined by the Game Master.

7.13 · Technique duration

A Technique producing a persistent effect must specify its duration.

For Area Techniques, the Builder provides the following options. They govern the persistence of Areas and Zones authorised by the Source; they do not replace the specific durations of Guard, Fury and Aim, or Grapple's per-turn upkeep.

DurationAdditional costHow it works
One turn0 STThe Technique sustains the effect for one turn.
Sustain1 ST per turnThe character pays upkeep to extend the effect.
Three turns2 STThe effect lasts three turns.
The whole sceneNo surcharge specifiedOption reserved for Grade d20 Techniques; Source requirements still apply.

Custom Techniques do not exceed d20. The requirement for the ‘Whole scene’ duration is d20 and does not grant the Technique a Prestige progression.

A Technique's duration does not automatically change the duration of Haki or Talents linked to it through a Special Move.

Each ability retains its own duration.

Example: a Zone can remain active for three turns, while a Talent used with the Technique may affect only the initial attack. The Zone's persistence does not extend the Talent.

7.14 · Weapons, Modules and Techniques

Weapons and Modules are the tools through which a character can perform certain Techniques.

A weapon does not generate a Technique: it performs it. The weapon's own effects are defined by the Weapon Builder and remain distinct from the Technique's effects.

A Style Technique can be linked to a compatible weapon or Module.

It cannot use both simultaneously as the means of performing the same Technique.

Weapons

To perform a Technique through a weapon, the character must meet its compatibility requirements, including those for Grade, Attribute and Style.

Striker Techniques are performed unarmed.

Crusher Techniques require a compatible blunt weapon.

Any special weapon effect applies under its own conditions and is not automatically transferred to the Technique merely because their two sheets are linked.

Modules

A Module must be installed, Intact and Active to perform a Technique requiring its active Function.

A Damaged Module cannot be used to perform the Technique until it is repaired.

If the Module is Inactive, the Cyborg must make it Active under the normal Module switching rules. An ordinary Active Module switch can be made for free once during their turn.

Making a Module Active remains distinct from activating Haki and Talents.

The Module provides the Technique's physical means and any properties specified on its sheet; it does not replace the Technique Die or duplicate effects already present in the Technique.

7.15 · Techniques and Special Moves

A built Technique can be selected as the basis of a Special Move.

The Special Move is a combination sheet: it links abilities already owned by the character and shows their formulas, costs, conditions and any preparations.

It does not modify the individual components' rules.

7.15.1 · Special Move components

A Special Move can include:

ComponentFunction
Base TechniqueDetermines the move performed and its effects.
One active TalentAdds the Talent's benefits when activated.
Passive TalentsAutomatically apply compatible benefits.
HakiProvides available benefits when active and their costs are paid.
Weapon or ModuleDetermines the equipment used to perform the move.

The components must belong to the character and meet all their own requirements.

A Special Move can contain at most one active Talent. Multiple active Talents cannot be linked to the same move to use them simultaneously.

7.15.2 · Action economy

Performing an offensive Special Move normally uses the main Action.

Activating an active Talent or Haki instead requires the Bonus Action.

Each turn, the character has only one Bonus Action and must choose how to use it.

It is therefore impossible to activate an active Talent and Haki simultaneously on the same turn.

Passive Talents require no activation and apply when their conditions are met.

7.15.3 · Preparing a Special Move with Haki and a Talent

A Special Move requiring both Haki and an active Talent may require preparation across multiple turns.

Example · Prepared attack

Turn 1

Haki activation

The character uses their Bonus Action to activate Haki, paying the specified cost.

The main Action remains available.

Turn 2

Performing the Special Move

The character keeps Haki active by paying any upkeep cost.

They use the Bonus Action to activate the Talent specified by the combination.

They use the main Action to perform the Special Move.

They apply the Technique's, active Haki's and compatible Talents' effects, observing their respective costs and limits.

If the Special Move requires Haki but no active Talent, the character can activate Haki with the Bonus Action and perform the Technique with the main Action in the same turn.

Preparing in advance does not allow a Talent to be kept beyond its natural duration.

7.15.4 · Costs and availability

Each component retains its own Stamina, PIP and other resource costs.

Haki must be active and have the required PIP available.

The active Talent must be available and observe its usage limit.

The weapon or Module must be usable.

If a component does not meet its own requirements, it cannot contribute to the Special Move.

A character can still perform the base Technique without using an unavailable additional ability, provided the Technique itself remains possible to perform.

7.15.5 · The Special Move sheet

A Special Move should clearly list the information needed to use it without consulting all the character's sheets each time.

Sheet · Special Move

Template

Name: combination name.

Base Technique: Technique used and its Grade.

Executor: weapon or Module, if present.

Active Talent: at most one.

Passive Talents: compatible abilities possessed.

Haki: Colour, selected effects and required state.

Preparation: any activations to perform on previous turns.

Action: use of the main Action to perform the move.

Costs: Stamina, PIP and other required resources.

Formula: attack roll, Damage Dice and any modifiers.

Conditions: reach, target, equipment and other requirements.

The sheet is not a new source of power: it is a way to prepare and quickly consult the character's combinations during combat.

Chapter 8 ·

Haki

There is a force that depends on neither muscles, weapons nor Devil Fruits. It is an individual's will taking shape, becoming perception, protection and, in the most extraordinary cases, a pressure capable of bending entire armies.

This force is called Haki.

In Grand Line Chronicles, Haki is a power the character develops during their adventure. It has a specific progression, a PIP reserve and abilities that can be used independently or linked to Special Moves.

Haki's power does not remove the need to prepare: a fighter must decide when to activate it, how long to sustain it and which effects to use.

8.1 · The three Haki Colours

There are three forms of Haki, called Colours.

Armament Haki

Busoshoku Haki

Hardens the body and weapons, enhances strikes and allows Devil Fruit defences to be countered.

Observation Haki

Kenbunshoku Haki

Allows presences to be perceived, attacks anticipated and, at higher levels, glimpses of the future.

Conqueror’s Haki

Haoshoku Haki

A rare manifestation of will, capable of dominating the battlefield and overwhelming weaker opponents.

Armament and Observation grow through dice Grades. Conqueror’s Haki instead has its own progression, divided into three levels.

8.2 · Obtaining and developing Haki

At character creation, Haki starts at None.

It may unlock during the campaign when the character's story and training justify it.

The Game Master decides when the character can acquire a Colour and authorises its progression under the advancement rules.

Armament and Observation

These two Colours follow the normal dice scale:

d6

1 PIP

d8

2 PIP

d10

3 PIP

d12

4 PIP

d20

5 PIP

The Grade determines unlocked abilities and the maximum PIP available. The Armament and Observation dice can never exceed the Spirit Die, including Prestige. Raising Spirit increases the attainable limit, but does not automatically increase the Colours. Beyond d20, progression continues under the Prestige Manual; the reserve remains 5 PIP for each Colour.

A character with Armament d10 also has the effects of Grades d6 and d8. They do not lose earlier abilities when developing the Colour.

Conqueror’s Haki

Conqueror’s Haki does not use a progression die.

It is an innate power the character must possess to develop. It cannot be obtained simply by declaring a desire to train it.

Its development is measured through three levels:

LevelName
1 PIPAwakening
2 PIPDominion
3 PIPAbsolute

Access to Conqueror’s Haki and its progression are established with the Game Master. Its Prestige has three distinct stages, assigned by the GM: Prestige I, Prestige II and Saikyō. It uses no die, requires no numerical Spirit thresholds and retains its ordinary maximum of 3 PIP.

8.3 · PIP

PIP represent the reserve of will the character can spend to activate and use Haki.

They are a resource separate from Stamina.

GradeMaximum PIP
d40
d61
d82
d103
d124
d205

This progression applies to Armament and Observation.

Conqueror’s Haki instead has a maximum reserve of 3 PIP, reached at Absolute level.

Each Colour has its own reserve. Armament PIP cannot be spent to pay for an Observation effect.

Recovering PIP

Spent PIP are recovered through a Short Rest.

Some specific effects, such as those of certain foods, may provide further recovery options.

PIP cannot be recovered beyond the maximum value of their reserve.

8.4 · Activating Haki

To use Haki when it requires an active state, the character must first activate the corresponding Colour.

Haki activation

1 Bonus Action

1 PIP

Activating a Haki Colour requires a Bonus Action and consumes 1 PIP from that Colour's reserve. The Colour Attunement Prestige exception allows two to be activated with the same Bonus Action, or three at Spirit Saikyō: each Colour still pays its own PIP. It does not also grant the use of an active Haki effect in the same turn.

Once active, the character can use its benefits according to unlocked abilities and their costs.

Haki activation does not consume the main Action.

A character can therefore activate Armament with the Bonus Action and make an attack with their Action in the same turn.

One Bonus Action per turn

Activating a Colour, an active Talent and an active Haki effect (⚡) compete for the same single Bonus Action. Activating the Colour and using one of its active effects require two separate Bonus Actions, and therefore different turns.

SituationAllowed?
Activate Haki and attackYes
Activate an active Talent and attackYes
Activate Haki and an active Talent in the same turnNo
Sustain Haki already active and activate a TalentYes

Two Bonus Actions cannot be used in the same turn.

If a combination requires both Haki activation and an active Talent, the character must prepare it by spreading the activations across several turns.

8.5 · Sustaining Haki

Once activated, Haki can be sustained on subsequent turns by paying its cost.

Ordinary upkeep

1 ST per turn

Sustaining Haki already active does not require a new Bonus Action.

A character sustaining Armament can use their Bonus Action to activate an active Talent.

Upkeep does not involve a new PIP activation cost: that PIP has already been paid.

If Haki ends and the character wants to use it again, they must activate it anew.

Exception · Armament d12

From Grade d12, once activated, Armament Haki no longer requires Stamina for upkeep.

It remains active until the character decides to stop it or an ability or condition causes it to end.

8.6 · Passive effects and active effects

Each Haki Grade can unlock two types of benefits.

Passive effects apply automatically when their respective conditions are met. They do not require a Bonus Action to be spent for each effect. If they depend on active Haki, the Colour must naturally already have been activated.

Each active effect (⚡) requires a player choice, 1 Bonus Action and payment of the listed PIP cost. It cannot be activated on the same turn as an active Talent, the first activation of a Colour or another active Haki effect.

Active effects must be declared before the roll they apply to, when a roll is required.

Using an effect of already active Haki during an attack does not require the entire Colour to be activated again.

Example

A character has Armament d10, and therefore 3 PIP.

On the first turn, they activate Haki with 1 Bonus Action and 1 PIP. They have 2 left.

On a subsequent turn, they sustain Haki by paying 1 ST and use the Bonus Action for Haki Burst, paying 1 PIP. They make the attack with the normal Action; their reserve falls to 1 PIP. They cannot also activate a Talent on the same turn.

If they sustain Haki on subsequent turns, they do not need to spend another activation PIP.

8.7 · Armament Haki

Busoshoku Haki

Armament Haki allows you to coat your body, weapons and attacks with your will.

It can be used both offensively and defensively, and also allows the normally intangible bodies of Logia users to be hit directly, unless specific abilities state otherwise.

When Armament is active, the character can apply their Armament Die to compatible uses.

Offensive Armament

When a physical attack is enhanced by Armament, the character adds their Armament Die to damage.

+1 Armament Die to damage

The attack need not be part of a Special Move. Armament can be applied normally to a basic attack, a Technique or any other compatible ability.

A Special Move does not grant a special use of Haki: it simply represents an organised combination of abilities the character already possesses.

Defensive Armament

Armament Haki is never rolled together with Passive Defence. Passive Defence remains a fixed value and does not automatically receive the Armament Die.

When the character makes a compatible Active Defence while Armament is active, they add their Armament Die to the roll.

Compatible Active Defence = Active Defence formula + Armament Die

Example:

Strength d10 + Athletics d8 + Armament d8

As with every Active Defence, a tie favours the defender.

Defence ≥ Attack → successful defence
Defence < Attack → the character is hit

Simply using the Armament Die requires no additional PIP beyond Haki activation. The special effects listed in the progression instead require their stated cost.

8.7.1 · d6: Competent

1 maximum PIP

Passive · Armament Control

Once activated, Armament can be sustained by paying:

1 ST per turn

Normal use of the Armament Die requires no further PIP. PIP are spent for Haki activation and special effects with a specific cost.

No additional active effect is available at this Grade.

8.7.2 · d8: Expert

2 maximum PIP

Passive · Condensed Armament

When the character rolls their Armament Die to determine damage, any result below half the die's maximum value is treated as equal to half.

  • Armament d8: results 1-3 count as 4.
  • Armament d10: results 1-4 count as 5.
  • Armament d12: results 1-5 count as 6.
  • Armament d20: results 1-9 count as 10.

Active · Full Armour

1 PIP

Defence

On their own turn, with Armament already active, the character prepares Full Armour by spending 1 Bonus Action and 1 PIP. They cannot activate it after being hit on an opponent's turn.

That single attack's damage is halved, rounding up.

Example: 13 damage becomes 7 damage.

The prepared protection applies to the next single attack hitting the character on subsequent enemy turns, while Armament remains active. It is consumed when it halves that damage and does not automatically protect against subsequent attacks. Preparing it again requires paying another Bonus Action and 1 PIP on their own turn.

8.7.3 · d10: Master

3 maximum PIP

Passive · Reinforced Defence

When the character uses their Armament Die in Active Defence, they roll the Armament Die twice and keep the better result. The other Active Defence dice are rolled as normal.

Active · Haki Burst

1 PIP

Offensive · Area

After an attack, the character can release a burst of Haki in an area of 5 metres.

Enemies reached by the burst take half the attack's damage.

The main target continues taking the original attack's full damage as normal.

8.7.4 · d12: Legend

4 maximum PIP

Passive · Armament Mastery

Once activated, Armament Haki no longer requires Stamina for upkeep.

It remains active until the character decides to stop it or an ability or condition causes it to end.

Active · Armament Armour

2 PIP

Defence · 2 turns

The character concentrates Armament over their entire body.

+4 Passive Defence for 2 turns

Example: Passive Defence 11 → Passive Defence 15.

The bonus modifies only Passive Defence. It does not add dice to Active Defence.

8.7.5 · d20: Mythic

5 maximum PIP

Passive · Perfect Armament

Whenever the character rolls their Armament Die, they roll it twice and keep the better result.

The benefit applies to offensive and defensive uses of the Armament Die. When used in Active Defence, it naturally replaces Reinforced Defence: the die is not rolled additional times.

Active · Ryou

3 PIP · Offensive

Armament penetrates outer defences and strikes the target from within. This is narrative and describes Ryou's manifestation: it does not create a separate mechanical category of internal damage.

When you apply Ryou to a compatible physical attack, calculate the attack's total damage as normal, including every die and bonus that actually applies, then multiply the result ×2.

Ryou doubles the total only once. Do not double individual components separately and then multiply the result again.

Ryou does not make the hit automatic or prevent the target from using Passive Defence, Active Defence, Damage Reduction or other normally allowed defences, unless a specific rule states otherwise.

8.8 · Observation Haki

Kenbunshoku Haki

Observation

Observation sharpens the fighter's perception. It allows them to recognise hidden presences, identify hostile intentions and react to dangers others cannot even see.

Observation uses the same Grades and PIP progression as Armament.

Its abilities focus on perception, defence and supporting companions.

8.8.1 · d6: Competent

1 maximum PIP

Passive · Observation Control

The character can keep Haki active by paying 1 ST per turn.

Abilities enhanced by Observation require no additional PIP unless a specific active effect is involved.

No additional special effect is available at this Grade yet.

8.8.2 · d8: Expert

2 maximum PIP

Passive · 360° Vision

The character cannot be surprised by ordinary attacks from behind.

Attacks from behind lose the benefit of surprise.

The passive effect does not automatically make the character immune to all forms of supernatural concealment or effects with specific rules.

Active · Anticipation

1 PIP

Defence

The character anticipates the opponent's movement.

Add two dice of their Observation Grade to a compatible defensive roll made for a Dodge or Active Defence performed with an attacking Technique. The benefit does not automatically grant damage: any retaliatory damage requires a Talent such as Counterblow and observes all its conditions, including exceeding the opponent's roll.

Example: with Observation d8, Anticipation adds 2d8 to the compatible defensive roll.

8.8.3 · d10: Master

3 maximum PIP

Passive · Reading the Field

The character perceives the position of every enemy in the scene and roughly recognises their Hit Points.

The GM can describe targets' conditions narratively: unhurt, wounded, badly wounded or near collapse.

The effect gives an approximate assessment, not necessarily every opponent's exact HP value.

Active · Nakama Guidance

1 PIP

Support · 1 turn

The character shares their perception of the battlefield with their companions.

For one turn, every companion in the character's line of sight adds that character's Observation Die as a bonus to their own rolls.

Example: a character with Observation d10 uses Nakama Guidance. For one turn, the affected companions receive an additional d10.

8.8.4 · d12: Legend

4 maximum PIP

Passive · Future Vision

Once per session

At the start of a scene, the player can ask the Game Master what is about to happen.

The GM provides a prediction consistent with imminent events.

The ability does not allow arbitrary knowledge of the entire plot or events outside the imminent scene.

Active · Brief Foresight

2 PIP

Defence · 1 turn

For one turn, ordinary physical attacks cannot hit the character.

Attacks using Haki and Area attacks remain effective under their respective rules.

The effect is therefore not absolute immunity to every kind of damage.

8.8.5 · d20: Mythic

5 maximum PIP

Passive · Universal Observation

The character perceives every living being on the island.

Enemies cannot hide or prepare an ambush without the character perceiving their presence.

The ability concerns perceiving presences, not automatic knowledge of their thoughts or detailed intentions.

Active · Future Foresight

3 PIP

2 turns

For two turns, the character anticipates combat with extraordinary precision.

During this period:

Attack: their attacks ignore defence.

Defence: automatically dodges every hit.

The effect lasts two turns and must be activated by paying 3 PIP.

Conflict between Future Foresights

When two characters have Future Foresight active simultaneously and their automatic effects directly conflict, the Future Foresight activated first takes precedence.

The effect activated later does not cancel the earlier one.

The second Foresight nevertheless remains active and continues functioning normally against other characters and in every situation where it does not conflict with the earlier one.

Example: if the first character activates Future Foresight and the second subsequently does the same, when the first attacks the second, their effect ignoring defence takes precedence. When the second attacks the first, the automatic dodge of the Foresight activated first takes precedence instead.

8.9 · Conqueror’s Haki

Haoshoku Haki

Conqueror’s Haki is a rare power tied to the character's innate will.

Its progression does not follow Armament and Observation dice. It has three levels, each characterised by passive abilities and, where specified, active effects.

Conqueror’s Haki has a maximum of 3 PIP.

Its passive effects apply automatically when the listed conditions occur, without spending PIP for each target.

Active effects instead require their costs to be paid. When an active effect requires the Colour to be already active, the normal Haki activation cost also applies.

8.9.1 · Level 1: Awakening

1 maximum PIP

Passive · Mass Stunning

The character's will overwhelms less resilient opponents.

All minor NPCs in the scene with HP equal to or below 15 are automatically Stunned.

No roll is required.

The effect does not automatically apply to important enemies and bosses simply because they have few HP: the target must belong to the category of minor NPCs specified by the ability.

This level grants no additional active effect.

8.9.2 · Level 2: Dominion

2 maximum PIP

Passive · Intimidating Aura

While enemies remain in the scene, those with Spirit below d8 suffer a penalty of:

−2 to their rolls

The benefit is automatic and requires no separate roll against each target.

Active · Emperor’s Shout

1 PIP

Once per combat

The character focuses their will on one opponent.

One chosen enemy loses their next Action.

The ability can also be used against a boss.

The usage limit is once per combat.

8.9.3 · Level 3: Absolute

3 maximum PIP

Passive · Battlefield Dominion

At the start of combat, the character's will overwhelms minor NPCs.

Their current HP are immediately halved, rounding down.

Example: a minor NPC has 15 HP. The effect reduces them to 7.

The ability applies only once at the start of combat, not repeatedly each turn.

Active · Conqueror’s Coating

2 PIP

Physical attack

The character concentrates Conqueror’s Haki on a single physical attack, coating it completely in their will.

The effect must be declared before making the attack roll.

If the attack hits:

  • the attack's Damage Dice are not rolled;
  • each Damage Die automatically takes its maximum result.

Examples:

  • 2d10 → 20 damage.
  • 3d12 → 36 damage.
  • 2d20 → 40 damage.

Any attack components that are not part of the Damage Dice follow their own rules as normal.

Conqueror’s Coating does not guarantee the attack hits: the attack roll and target's defence are resolved as normal.

At Absolute Level, activating Conqueror’s Haki costs 1 PIP and Conqueror’s Coating costs 2: using both consumes the entire 3-PIP reserve. The character must therefore recover PIP through a Short Rest before using Conqueror’s Coating again.

8.10 · Haki and Devil Fruits

Armament Haki allows the defences normally offered by Devil Fruits to be countered.

Its most important interaction concerns Logia.

A Logia user can normally allow ordinary attacks to pass through their elemental body.

Armament instead allows their body to be hit, overcoming this ordinary intangibility.

This does not mean every Haki hit automatically cancels all the target's powers.

The Fruit's specific abilities, defensive Techniques and advanced effects explicitly providing different interactions still apply.

Haki also does not remove the rules for other Fruit weaknesses, such as the sea and Seastone.

8.11 · Haki and Special Moves

A Special Move can use one or more benefits of the character's Haki.

The Special Move does not activate Haki for free or change the costs of selected effects.

To apply a benefit requiring active Haki, the corresponding Colour must have been activated.

To apply an effect costing PIP, the character must have and spend the necessary PIP.

8.11.1 · Special Move with Haki only

If a Special Move requires neither a Talent's activation nor an active Haki effect (⚡), the character can activate the Colour with the Bonus Action and perform the move with the ordinary Action in the same turn. To use a ⚡ effect, the Colour must already be active from a previous turn.

8.11.2 · Special Move with Haki and an active Talent

If a Special Move requires both, the character must spread their activations across several turns.

8.12 · Using multiple Haki effects

Passive effects already unlocked apply when their respective conditions are met.

Active effects consume the PIP listed in their descriptions and each requires 1 Bonus Action. Only one effect can be activated in the turn, as an alternative to an active Talent or activating a Colour.

The character must declare which effects they are using and pay their total cost.

An effect from a Grade not yet reached cannot be used.

Example · Armament d20

A character has 5 PIP.

On the first turn, they activate Haki with 1 Bonus Action and 1 PIP. Their reserve drops to 4.

On a subsequent turn, while sustaining Haki, they use the Bonus Action for Ryou and pay 3 PIP. They make the attack with the ordinary Action and are left with 1 PIP. They cannot activate a Talent on the same turn or save Ryou for an attack on a later turn.

Activating other effects requires their PIP and the Bonus Action of another turn. Multiple active benefits can coexist only if prepared separately and still within their duration: for example, an effect lasting 2 turns can accompany a Talent activated on the next turn, provided it has not expired. PIP already spent are not paid again and the duration is not renewed.

The activation cost and special effect costs are separate.

Colour activation and an active effect must happen on different turns because each requires its own Bonus Action. Colour upkeep and passive effects, instead, do not require a new Bonus Action.

8.13 · Ending Haki and recovery

An already active Haki Colour does not end merely because its PIP reserve reaches 0, even when the last PIP was spent to activate it.

While the character pays the Stamina required for upkeep, the Colour and its ordinary benefits remain active. If the Grade reached removes the upkeep cost, the already activated Colour can remain active even at 0 PIP without spending ST.

At 0 PIP, you cannot activate an inactive Colour or activate new effects requiring PIP. An effect already paid for retains only the duration and uses specified in its description: exhausting the reserve neither renews it nor automatically cancels it.

A Colour ends when:

  • the character decides not to sustain it;
  • they do not pay the required upkeep cost;
  • they lose consciousness;
  • they suffer a Status or effect preventing them from sustaining it, such as Stunned.

When Haki ends, benefits requiring the active Colour cease. Effects with an independent duration follow their own descriptions. Reactivating the Colour normally requires 1 Bonus Action and 1 PIP.

PIP are recovered through a Short Rest or abilities explicitly providing recovery, without exceeding the Colour's maximum. Haki does not replace Stamina: upkeep, Techniques and Talents retain their own costs.

Chapter 9 ·

Devil Fruits

A single bite can change a pirate's fate.

Some become rubber, some transform into a legendary creature and others gain the power of a natural element. No Fruit is identical to another, but all share one feature: they grant extraordinary abilities in exchange for permanent vulnerability to the sea.

In Grand Line Chronicles, Devil Fruits are customisable powers. The player defines their concept with the Game Master, while the Fruit Die, Talents and Technique Builder govern their growth.

The Fruit does not replace the character's Combat Style. It adds to their abilities, allowing them to develop a unique way of fighting.

Devil Fruit Manual. This chapter summarises Fruit rules. The Fruit Identity Sheet, the explanation and definitive clarification of every Talent, Trait and Signature, and the guidelines for Combat Form and Awakening are in the Devil Fruit Manual (Library · Volume 04): consult that manual for more detail; it takes precedence in the event of differences.

9.1 · Obtaining a Devil Fruit

Having a Devil Fruit is optional and requires the Game Master's approval.

The character can start the campaign with a Fruit or obtain one during the adventure.

When acquiring the power, the player must define its fundamental characteristics.

CharacteristicDescription
NameFruit name.
TypeParamecia, Logia or Zoan.
DescriptionNature of the power and its possibilities.
Fruit DieGrade of mastery over the power.
TraitFundamental ability associated with the type.
SignatureEffect triggered under the specified conditions.
TalentsAbilities acquired through growth.

The Fruit need not be combat-oriented.

An apparently modest power may prove useful in exploration, navigation, creating objects or social interactions.

Its effectiveness depends on the applications the character develops.

9.2 · The three Fruit types

Devil Fruits belong to three categories.

Paramecia

The unique power

Grants an extraordinary property to the body, allows a substance to be produced or modifies matter and the environment.

Logia

The element

Allows the user to become and control a natural element, gaining its properties.

Zoan

Transformation

Allows an animal form and a hybrid form to be assumed, changing the body and physical abilities.

Each type has a specific Trait, Signature and Talent Tree.

Mythical and Ancient Fruits belong to the Zoan category, but may gain special characteristics through the abilities in their Tree.

9.3 · Weaknesses and interactions

Devil Fruit users share two fundamental weaknesses: the sea and Agalmatolite, known as Seastone.

The sea

When they fall into water and remain submerged, the user loses all strength, cannot swim and is left helpless. They cannot take main Actions, Bonus Actions or Reactions, or make voluntary Movement. They need to be rescued.

A Fish-Man or Merman can continue breathing underwater and does not drown, but still loses the ability to swim and remains helpless on the seabed until freed from the water.

Agalmatolite · Seastone

Contact with Seastone deprives the user of their actions, like immersion. Contact suffered during an opponent's turn makes them lose the actions of their next turn, including their main Action, Bonus Action and Reactions. If contact persists, the character continues losing all actions and cannot move voluntarily until freed.

A single contact does not impose permanent paralysis: once the next turn has been lost, if they are no longer touching Seastone, the character can act again. Continuous contact instead renews the condition.

Haki and interaction with Fruits

Haki is not a weakness equivalent to the sea or Seastone. Armament allows Logia bodies to be hit despite ordinary intangibility, under the Colour's rules, but does not automatically remove actions or indiscriminately negate all Fruit properties or powers.

9.4 · The Fruit Die

The Fruit Die represents the character's level of mastery.

It does not determine which power they possess, but how well they can control it.

An inexperienced user might produce their element without being able to shape it precisely. An expert might instead employ it in completely different ways.

Progression follows this scale:

DieMastery
d4The power has manifested.
d6The character begins to understand it.
d8Reliable control.
d10Advanced mastery.
d12Mastery.
d20The pinnacle of the power, required for Awakening.

The Fruit Die normally starts at d4.

9.4.1 · How it grows

The Fruit Die is not increased by spending Ability Points.

The Game Master determines its advancement when the character's growth justifies it.

An improvement may be linked to training, a discovery about how the Fruit works, a significant defeat or the end of a story arc.

The die should not increase automatically after every combat.

9.4.2 · What it determines

The Fruit Die serves two functions.

Limits Fruit Techniques: no Technique using the Fruit as its Source can exceed its Grade.

Unlocks Fruit Talents: each Tree tier requires a minimum mastery.

Talent progression

d4–d6 · The Gift
The character explores the power's initial possibilities.

d8–d10 · Control
The Fruit becomes more versatile and efficient.

d12 · Mastery
The character develops advanced applications.

d20 · Awakening
Reaching the power's highest manifestation becomes possible.

9.5 · Building Fruit Techniques

Fruit Techniques use the Builder in Chapter 7, but the Fruit is a special Source. Effects must be consistent with the Power’s Core and Identity Sheet. When the Fruit's nature permits it, the GM can authorise effects, Areas, Ranges or Persistent Zones outside a Style's ordinary catalogue. Authorisation does not remove Grade, slots or ST costs: a Fruit must be able to actually produce the result.

To create one, the player selects Fruit as the Source and defines its Attribute, Form, Grade and effects.

The Technique must meet three fundamental conditions:

Its Grade cannot exceed the Die of the Attribute used.

Its Grade cannot exceed the Fruit Die.

The chosen effects must be consistent with the power's nature and the Builder rules.

The Game Master checks the Technique's narrative compatibility.

A Fruit capable of producing wax can create barriers and structures. It cannot freely produce lightning merely because the Catalogue contains an effect suited to an electrical attack.

d4 and d6 Techniques

d4 and d6 Techniques have 0 Effect slots and cannot select effects taking slots unless an ability explicitly provides an exception.

Effects taking slots normally become available from Grade d8, observing their requirements. Source-authorised templates take no slots, but retain their own minimum Grade and cost (§7.9).

This limit concerns built Techniques. It does not remove the Fruit's natural Trait or the abilities of Talents already possessed.

Costs

Fruit Techniques pay the Stamina required by their effects.

Having a Fruit does not automatically make all Techniques derived from it free.

Specific Talents can reduce or remove certain costs.

When an effect included in a Fruit Technique applies a Status granting a Save, its Threshold is determined by the Technique's Grade, not directly by the Fruit Die (§1.4 of the Game Manual).

9.6 · Fruit Talents

Each Fruit type has a dedicated Tree.

Talents are acquired through Upgrades, choosing among those available based on the Fruit Die and observing any prerequisites.

Reaching a Grade unlocks access to Talents, but does not automatically grant all those in the tier.

Talents can represent passive abilities, voluntary activations, Reactions or particular exceptions.

In the V2 system, a Talent requiring voluntary activation normally uses the Bonus Action. When its description specifies a Reaction, free activation or another explicit procedure, that rule takes precedence.

Passive effects apply automatically when their conditions are met.

Every Fruit Talent is explained and clarified in the Devil Fruit Manual, which takes precedence over this summary in case of doubt.

9.7 · Paramecia

Paramecia

The unique power

Paramecia include extremely diverse powers. They can alter the body, produce matter or affect the environment.

Their fundamental principle is creative freedom: the player and Game Master define a recognisable power and develop its applications without turning it into an unlimited collection of unrelated abilities.

9.7.1 · Trait: The Unique Power

Always active

The character alters body, matter or the environment through a power agreed with the Game Master.

The Fruit's nature determines what the character can do.

There is no universal table assigning every Paramecia the same set of effects.

The Trait represents the Fruit's fundamental properties, not an unlock to purchase.

9.7.2 · Signature: The Power’s Mark

Clean Hit · Margin +4

When the character achieves a Clean Hit with a Fruit Technique, the power leaves an additional consequence consistent with its nature.

It may be an alteration to the terrain, a Status or a zone that remains after impact.

The actual application is defined with the Game Master according to the power possessed.

The Signature does not grant the player permanent access to every Catalogue effect: it represents the Clean Hit's special consequence.

If the Signature's consequence is a Status granting a Save, the Threshold is determined by the Fruit Die (§1.4 of the Game Manual), because the Status is produced by the Signature and not selected as a Technique effect. The Technique's Clean Hit is still required.

9.7.3 · d4–d6: The Gift

Versatile Power

Passive

Unlock a new Fruit application.

You might learn to use the power at a distance, apply it to your body or employ it for defence.

The new application allows an entire family of Techniques to be developed that you could not previously build.

The choice must be consistent with the Fruit's original concept.

When the Talent is acquired, the new Application is chosen together with the GM and recorded on the Fruit Identity Sheet: it must follow logically from the Power’s Core.

The Talent grants only one new direction, not all of them at once, and does not allow new properties to be invented each time it is used.

Fine Control

Passive · Outside combat

Add +1 die to appropriate manipulation, traversal, protection and stealth checks when you use the Fruit.

The bonus applies only to activities in which the power actually provides an advantage.

Fine Control grants no new Applications: the character must already be able to perform the action according to their Fruit Sheet.

Passive Gift

Passive · Permanent

The Fruit grants you a permanent property consistent with its nature.

This property requires neither Stamina nor the use of a Technique.

For example, a rubber body does not conduct electricity.

The player and GM agree exactly which property is gained and what consequences it produces.

Once defined, it remains a permanent characteristic of the character.

The property is chosen when the Talent is acquired, recorded on the Fruit Sheet and must follow directly from the power's nature.

It cannot amount to a generic immunity to damage, permanent Attribute bonuses, existing Talents or abilities that do not truly follow from the nature of the Fruit. Once chosen, it does not change from one scene to another.

Power Reflex

Reaction

1 time per battle

0 ST

When you or an ally within 5 metres are about to take damage from an attack, the Fruit reacts instinctively.

All of the attack's Damage Dice are reduced by:

1 Grade

Progression:

d20 → d12
d12 → d10
d10 → d8
d8 → d6
d6 → d4

d4 is the minimum.

Power Reflex modifies Damage Dice only.

It modifies the attack's Damage Dice, not the attack roll, and does not cancel Statuses, Displacements or other attack effects unless they expressly depend on damage.

9.7.4 · d8–d10: Control

Dual Use

2 ST

Active Talent · Bonus Action

During the same Action, you can perform two different applications of your Fruit.

Only one of the two can be an attack or directly produce an offensive effect.

The second application must be narrative or useful and may, for example, allow you to manipulate an object, produce a small amount of your substance, change an element of the scene or use another non-offensive property of the Fruit.

Dual Use does not grant two attacks, two offensive Techniques or two separate applications of damage during the same Action.

The Talent costs 2 ST only once.

Any Technique used pays its own costs normally.

The second application pays a separate cost only if the ability used already specifies one.

What Remains

Passive

The durations of Fruit Techniques improve at no additional cost.

Original durationBenefit
One turnBecomes three turns, without paying the additional 2 ST.
SustainThe first turn of upkeep is free.

The Talent modifies only the durations to which it applies.

What Remains modifies only a mechanical duration specified by the Technique and does not make what the Fruit creates permanent: if the Technique has neither a duration nor upkeep, the Talent provides no benefit.

The natural permanence of matter recorded on the Fruit Sheet remains a separate property.

None of My Own

Passive

The Eye of the Storm effect becomes more accessible for your Fruit Techniques. It does not grant the Area Form on its own: it applies only to a Technique that already has a valid Area according to the nature of the power and the Source's rules.

When you use it:

It takes up no slots.

It costs no Stamina.

It can be selected even before the normal d12 requirement.

The Fruit recognises the character's companions and allows them to be excluded from the effects of their Area Techniques.

The player chooses which targets in the Area are excluded. The Talent protects against the direct consequences of the Technique, not independent environmental consequences that may arise later in the scene.

9.7.5 · d12: Mastery

Natural Reach

Passive

Your Fruit has reached such a level of control that projecting the power becomes more efficient.

You gain three benefits:

Range effects for Fruit Techniques are free and take up no slots.

Area Techniques increase their radius by one step.

The Chain effect costs 1 ST less.

Natural Reach does not create a Range, an Area or a Chain that the Fruit could not produce. The benefits apply only to Fruit Techniques already authorised by the Source; they remove the cost of the Range actually selected without granting it automatically.

Natural Reach modifies Techniques, not the ordinary Reach on the Fruit Sheet, and does not allow free narrative uses beyond that Reach.

Area Techniques increase by one step on the normal template progression; Chain does not fall below the normal applicable minimum.

Combat Form

Active Talent · Bonus Action

The character continuously uses their Fruit on themselves, turning one of its properties into a personal mode of combat.

When the Talent is acquired, player and Game Master define:

Manifestation

How the power changes the character's body, movements or fighting style.

Benefit

The mechanical advantage gained while the Form is active.

Cost

The Stamina required to activate or maintain it.

Limitations

Any conditions required to use the Form.

The benefit must follow from an ability the Fruit already possesses and must primarily concern the character themselves.

For example, it may improve mobility, offensive capability, defensive capability or another personal characteristic consistent with the power.

Combat Form grants no new Fruit Application and does not normally allow the environment to be changed, target categories to be expanded or important structural Limitations to be bypassed.

Once defined, the Form remains unchanged and cannot change its benefit each time it is used.

Normally, the Form can remain active for the entire scene as long as the character pays its cost: if they can no longer pay the required upkeep, it ends.

The complete guideline for building a Combat Form can be found in the Devil Fruits Manual.

No Backlash

Passive

Fruit Techniques cost:

1 ST less

to a minimum of:

1 ST.

In addition, you no longer suffer the natural Backlashes recorded on the Fruit Identity Sheet.

No Backlash does not remove:

  • structural Limitations;
  • contact or visibility requirements;
  • Reach;
  • maximum quantities;
  • upkeep;
  • Technique conditions;
  • costs belonging to other Talents;
  • the fundamental weaknesses of Devil Fruits.

No Backlash removes what the Fruit involuntarily does to its user: it does not remove costs deliberately specified by the rules or consequences caused by enemies or the environment.

Instinctive Power

Once per scene

Exception to Actions

You can use a Fruit move without spending your main Action, even outside your turn.

The ability modifies the Action cost of the move, but does not automatically remove its Stamina cost.

The move continues to pay Stamina, any PIP or other resources, upkeep, requirements and conditions normally.

Instinctive Power does not grant an additional turn, Movement, a second Bonus Action or a second Reaction: it is only an exception to the Action cost of that single move.

Second Nature

Prerequisite: Versatile Power

Passive

The Fruit reveals a second family of applications, completely different from the first.

The new possibility is agreed with the Game Master.

It does not necessarily represent an increase in the numerical power of the Fruit: it expands its possibilities.

Example: a power initially used to change your own body could develop a consistent mode that also acts on external materials, if the concept of the Fruit allows it.

Versatile Power grants a new Application; Second Nature grants a new family of Applications, recorded on the Identity Sheet and usable as a narrative basis for new Techniques.

Second Nature does not automatically grant Catalogue effects, damage, Statuses or bonuses.

9.7.6 · d20: Awakening

Awakening · Paramecia

Ultimate

Requirements: Fruit Die d20, Ambition and permission from the Game Master.

Awakening represents the ultimate evolution of the Power’s Core.

The fundamental principle of the Paramecia reaches a scale, freedom or depth that is normally impossible.

Awakening must be completely defined before its first activation using the Awakening Guideline in the Devil Fruits Manual.

The following must be established:

  • Name;
  • Awakened Principle;
  • Manifestation;
  • Families involved;
  • Agreed Benefits;
  • Area or Reach, where relevant;
  • Valid targets;
  • Permanence;
  • Limitations overcome;
  • Limitations still in force.

The available Families are:

  • Origin
  • Costs / Discounts
  • Expansion
  • Domain
  • Defence
  • Attack
  • Control

The Families do not automatically grant an effect.

They indicate which aspect of the Fruit is taken beyond its normal limits.

Awakening does not replace Techniques.

When a manifestation is intended to produce damage, Statuses, Zones, Displacements or other effects governed by the system, it must use an appropriate Technique or have an Awakening Benefit that expressly specifies that consequence.

Awakening normally lasts:

1 scene.

At the end of the scene:

  • Stamina falls to 0;
  • the character cannot use the Fruit until their next Long Rest.

Voluntarily ending Awakening before the end of the scene does not avoid these consequences.

9.8 · Logia

Logia

The element

Logia allow you to become and control a natural element.

The nature of the element permanently influences the character's body and their possibilities for movement, attack and defence.

9.8.1 · Trait: The Element

Always active

The character becomes and controls their element.

They can use it to strike, even at range, and their attacks naturally possess its characteristics.

The elemental nature of the Fruit:

It costs no Stamina.

Takes up no Effect slots.

Does not have to be purchased through the Builder.

If the character is made of fire, their attacks naturally possess the characteristics of fire. There is no need to buy narrative permission to produce it separately.

Intangibility

Attacks without Haki or Seastone can pass through the elemental body.

Depending on the nature of the attack and the situation, they may miss completely or deal half damage.

The GM determines which of the two consequences is appropriate.

Intangibility belongs to the initial Logia Trait. You do not need to reach an advanced Grade to gain it.

9.8.2 · Signature: The Element Spreads

Clean Hit · Margin +4

When you score a Clean Hit using the Fruit, the element spreads over an entire zone.
