The manifestation of the effect must respect the nature of the element and the situation.

The Signature does not automatically assign numerical dimensions or costs that the ability does not specify.

9.8.3 · d4–d6: The Gift

Sense the Element

Passive

You perceive whatever comes into contact with your element.

You can recognise the presence, position and movement of anyone within it even without seeing them directly.

Perception works through:

  • your elemental body;
  • the element you are producing;
  • the element you are directly controlling.

An external source of your element does not automatically become part of your perception.

To establish a connection to an external source, you must at least:

be able to see it.

Once you have brought it under your control, you can perceive whatever comes into contact with it as long as the connection remains valid.

Sense the Element does not allow you to perceive automatically through every source of your element in the scene or the world.

Elemental Body

Passive

Add +1 die to escape, mobility and breaking-free checks when you exploit the nature of your elemental body.

The bonus applies only when the nature of the element actually provides an advantage. Elemental Body does not grant immunity to Grapples or immobilisation: that ability belongs to Dispersed Body.

Always in Form

Passive

Your intangibility becomes instinctive.

It continues to work even when you are caught by surprise, asleep or unconscious.

The Fruit's weaknesses still apply.

The Talent modifies when intangibility works, not its power, and does not change the normal interactions with Haki, Seastone and the sea.

Feed on the Element

Passive

When you are near a living source of your element, you recover 1d6 Stamina at the start of each scene.

The source must be sufficiently significant and consistent with the Fruit.

A fire user could feed on a blaze or a furnace. An ice user could draw on a glacier.

A mere trace of your element does not necessarily count as a living source.

Recovery occurs only once, at the start of the scene. A source created specifically by the user is not considered valid.

9.8.4 · d8–d10: Control

Dispersed Body

Passive

Your elemental body cannot be grabbed, restrained, tied up or immobilised using ordinary hands, ropes and chains.

You can also pass through any opening through which your element could pass.

For example, a smoke Logia could pass through the gaps in a door.

Haki and Seastone remain effective. This Talent does not remove the weaknesses of the Fruit.

It does not allow you to pass through a completely airtight wall: the possibility depends on the properties of the element.

Absorb

Passive

When you suffer an attack made of your own element, you take no damage.

Instead, you recover 1d6 Stamina.

The Talent requires the attack to actually use the element corresponding to your Fruit.

Mere affinity is not enough: a fire Logia absorbs fire, not automatically magma, explosions or lightning. The Fruit Sheet establishes what truly belongs to the element.

Absorb cannot be deliberately exploited through attacks by the character themselves or an ally made solely to generate Stamina.

Omnipresent Element

Passive

Your Fruit Techniques no longer have to originate from your body.

They can manifest through a source of your element in the scene.

The source must:

  • actually consist of your element;
  • be present in the scene;
  • be visible at the moment it is used.

When you use a source this way, it becomes the point of origin of the Technique.

Where relevant, line of fire, cover, direction, Area and distances are determined from the source.

Omnipresent Element does not automatically grant permanent control of every source of your element in the scene.

The Talent does not remove the costs of the Technique or the requirements of its effects.

You cannot use a hidden source merely because the character knows it exists.

Reform Elsewhere

Reaction · Grade d10 · Once per encounter · 15 metres

When an immediate, perceptible event occurs to which the character can reasonably react, they can use their Reaction to dissolve their body and reform at a point they can see within 15 metres.

Reform Elsewhere has no single universal trigger. It can be used in response, for example, to a declared attack, a sudden danger, a collapse, an explosion, hostile movement or another event that actually leaves room for a Reaction. The Game Master determines case by case whether the event is immediate and perceptible enough to allow its use.

The displacement does not require the main Action and does not provoke Reactions caused by displacement. The Talent does not consume the Bonus Action: it uses the Reaction.

The destination must be visible and actually reachable by the Logia manifestation; Reform Elsewhere does not automatically allow conditions or obstacles to be crossed that the nature of the element would not allow you to overcome.

9.8.5 · d12: Mastery

Elemental Domain

Passive

The character gains advanced control over the element already present in the environment.

They can influence its properties and distribution to change the conditions of the scene.

Applications include manipulating terrain, creating or modifying cover, feeding or extinguishing elemental sources and using environmental resources.

The Talent expands control over the element, but does not automatically assign numerical damage, dimensions or durations.

When an application produces a mechanical combat effect, use the rules of the corresponding Technique or those agreed with the Game Master.

Purely narrative changes can be made through the Talent. Elemental Domain does not allow other matter to be turned into your element.

Whoever Touches You

Passive · Defence

The nature of your element is dangerous even to those who manage to hit you.

When an enemy hits you in melee bare-handed or using another part of their body, they take:

1d6

Damage from your element

The damage must be consistent with the nature of the Fruit: burns for fire, freezing for ice, and so on.

The Talent does not automatically apply to an enemy who hits you from a distance or through a weapon without making contact with your body.

The damage applies once for each attack that produces direct physical contact, regardless of the number of blows described.

If contact with that element cannot reasonably cause damage, the Talent may not be appropriate for that specific Logia: the GM does not invent a harmful property the element does not possess.

Inexhaustible Source

Prerequisite: Feed on the Element

Passive

When you are inside or immediately beside a large source of your element, your Fruit Techniques cost no Stamina.

The benefit concerns Fruit Techniques and requires the source to actually be available.

A small or purely decorative source does not automatically meet the condition.

Example: a fire Logia fighting inside a large blaze could use their Fruit Techniques for free as long as they remain in the required condition.

A source deliberately created by the character does not automatically become valid. The benefit does not remove costs of Talents, Haki or other resources.

Lost Form

Active Talent · Bonus Action to activate

The character completely disperses their body into the element.

They can take the form of a cloud, a current, a spreading flame or another consistent manifestation.

The transformation lasts an entire scene.

While in this state:

They cannot wield or use objects.

They cannot make attacks.

They can move anywhere their element can reach.

They can pass through barriers that are not airtight.

They cannot be recognised by their normal appearance.

Returning to ordinary form requires a main Action.

Lost Form is a state of dispersion and mobility, not an offensive Technique. It does not allow you to attack while also retaining the benefits of complete dispersion.

Moving anywhere the element can reach describes possible paths, not unlimited movement: the character still respects their Movement. While in Lost Form, they cannot use offensive Techniques.

9.8.6 · d20: Awakening

Awakening · Logia

Ultimate

Requirements: Fruit Die d20, Ambition and permission from the Game Master.

Awakening represents the ultimate manifestation of the Logia's element.

During Awakening, the element invades and changes the surrounding environment.

Logia Awakening is built using the normal Awakening Guideline.

Elemental Saturation

Every Logia Awakening necessarily possesses Elemental Saturation.

The element invades the Area defined on the Awakening Sheet and progressively changes the conditions of the environment.

The concrete manifestation depends on the nature of the element.

The Area has no universal size and is defined when the Awakening is built.

Hostile Environment

Elemental Saturation makes the environment dangerous for creatures present.

When the Awakening is designed, the Conditions or environmental consequences appropriate to the nature of the element are defined.

Where consistent, they may concern:

  • visibility;
  • movement;
  • breathing;
  • temperature;
  • perception;
  • other consequences inherent to the element.

The effects are established before use and cannot be freely changed at each activation.

When a Condition normally allows a Save, it continues to apply.

Developing Awakening

In addition to Elemental Saturation and Hostile Environment, Awakening is developed using the normal Families:

  • Origin
  • Costs / Discounts
  • Expansion
  • Domain
  • Defence
  • Attack
  • Control

The concrete Benefits are defined beforehand through the Awakening Guideline.

Awakening normally lasts:

1 scene.

At the end:

  • Stamina falls to 0;
  • the character cannot use the Fruit until their next Long Rest.

Voluntarily ending Awakening early does not avoid the required price.

9.9 · Zoan

Zoan

Transformation

Zoan allow the character to transform into an animal creature and combine the characteristics of the animal with those of their own body.

Unlike Paramecia and Logia, their strength is primarily physical: strength, resilience, mobility, senses and the natural characteristics of the animal.

Every Zoan has three fundamental forms.

Human

The character's ordinary appearance.

Hybrid

Combines a human body and animal characteristics.

Beast

Takes the complete form of the animal.

9.9.1 · Trait: The Three Forms

Free

The character can transform into their animal form and hybrid form.

The fundamental transformation:

It costs no Stamina.

Takes up no Effect slots.

Does not require a Technique to be created.

A normal transformation uses the main Action; you can return to Human Form from Hybrid or Beast Form.

In hybrid or beast form, the body takes on the size and characteristics of the corresponding creature.

Specific additional abilities are determined by the Talents acquired.

The Three Forms Talent further governs free switching between forms during combat.

9.9.2 · Signature: The Overwhelming Blow

Clean Hit · Margin +4

When the character scores a Clean Hit while in hybrid form, they can choose one of the following benefits:

Stun

The target becomes Stunned.

Additional damage

Add a Damage Die to the attack.

The character chooses one of the two effects, not both.

The Signature expressly requires hybrid form: it does not automatically apply to human or beast form.

9.9.3 · d4–d6: The Gift

Hybrid Form

Active Talent · Bonus Action

Take a form that combines the human and animal characteristics of the Fruit.

For the entire scene you gain:

+1 die to physical rolls based on Strength and Athletics.

+1 Damage Die to melee attacks.

The damage benefit applies to melee attacks compatible with the form.

The Talent does not automatically modify all of the character's Attributes.

Hybrid Form can be taken even without this Talent: the Talent represents the ability to use it as a true combat configuration.

Animal Instinct

Passive

You exploit the instincts of the creature into which you can transform.

During exploration, add +1 die to appropriate Observation, Survival and pursuit checks.

You can also overcome prohibitive terrain that would stop an ordinary character when the characteristics of the animal justify it.

An animal capable of climbing, for example, can tackle surfaces that would require specific equipment for others.

The bonus applies only when the instincts of the creature actually provide an advantage. Animal Instinct does not replace Animal Senses.

Three Forms

Free

1 time per turn

You can freely switch between human, hybrid and beast form once per turn, without consuming the main Action.

The change can occur even during a sequence of blows.

The Talent grants no new Actions and does not allow unlimited transformations in the same turn.

Changing form uses the Bonus Action and costs no Stamina.

Claws and Fangs

Passive

The natural offensive characteristics of the Zoan become true Natural Weapons.

They may include claws, fangs, horns, a beak, a tail, spikes, quills, pincers, stingers or other structures consistent with the creature.

When you acquire the Talent, record the Natural Weapons available in the appropriate forms.

For each Natural Weapon indicate:

  • Name
  • Anatomical part
  • Available form
  • Weapon type
  • Grade
  • Attribute
  • Reach

The Grade of the Natural Weapon equals the:

Fruit Die.

Each Natural Weapon is associated with one of the normal weapon types:

Blade

Claws, fangs and cutting structures.

Blunt

Hooves, headbutts, heavy tails and impact structures.

Polearm

Long horns, stingers and piercing structures.

Ranged

Quills or projectiles produced directly by the body.

Thrown

Anatomical parts that can be detached and thrown.

Special

Tongues, prehensile tails, tentacles or other unusual anatomy.

If the Natural Weapon works at range, its Reach must be recorded.

Natural Weapons take up no places in the Arsenal and can be used to make basic attacks even by characters who do not have the Fighter Role.

They do not require a Combat Style to make a basic attack and cannot normally be disarmed.

The Talent does not automatically grant Techniques or Talents belonging to Combat Styles.

The classification is chosen when the Natural Weapon is recorded and does not change from one attack to another. Melee Natural Weapons normally use the reach appropriate to the Form and Size of the Zoan.

9.9.4 · d8–d10: Control

Animal Senses

Passive

The characteristic senses of the creature reach an exceptional level.

When you acquire the Talent, the senses appropriate to your creature are identified.

They may include:

  • smell;
  • hearing;
  • sight;
  • echolocation;
  • vibration perception;
  • thermal perception;
  • other natural sensory abilities.

Through these senses you can locate hidden creatures within:

20 metres

even without seeing them directly, provided the sense used can actually detect them.

You can also follow days-old tracks when your sense allows it.

The Talent does not automatically grant every possible sense.

Use only those consistent with the creature.

Beast Run

Passive · Beast Form

Beast Form makes full use of its natural mode of locomotion.

When you acquire the Talent, assign the Zoan to a Movement Family.

FamilyExamplesIncrease
Land sprinterFelines, fast canines, equines, antelopes, ostriches, bipedal predators+4 m on the ground
Heavy land animalBears, bovines, boars, rhinoceroses, elephants, large heavy dinosaurs+2 m on the ground
ClimberPrimates, goats, geckos, tree-dwelling animals+2 m normally; +4 m when climbing or moving appropriately
FlierBirds, bats, winged insects, pterosaurs+2 m on the ground; +4 m when flying
BurrowerMoles, badgers, insects and burrowing creatures+2 m on the ground; +4 m when burrowing appropriately
SerpentineSnakes and large slithering creatures+3 m during compatible displacements

In addition, you ignore natural Difficult Terrain when the creature's mode of locomotion is appropriate to the situation.

The chosen Family remains associated with the Fruit.

The weaknesses of Devil Fruits continue to apply normally in water.

The Family cannot be freely changed. There is no aquatic Movement Family: a Zoan based on an aquatic creature possesses its anatomy and characteristics, but immersion continues to produce the normal consequences for Devil Fruits.

Size

Passive · Beast Form

The mass and dimensions of Beast Form become a concrete advantage during combat.

SizeIndicative dimensionsReach
Small / Mediumup to approximately 2.5 m1 m
Largeapproximately 2.5–5 m2 m
Hugeapproximately 5–8 m3 m
Giganticapproximately 8–12 m4 m
Colossalover 12 m5 m

The dimensions shown are game references.

The actual Beast Form determines the appropriate Size.

When you move through the space occupied by a creature smaller than your Size, you can trample it and make it:

Off-Balance.

A Zoan of Large Size or above can also carry at least one normally sized character without reducing their Movement.

Size does not artificially increase the dimensions of Beast Form.

Melee attacks made through the body or Natural Weapons use the Reach specified by Size when it is greater than the weapon's normal reach.

The Talent does not allow you to trample creatures of the same or greater Size. A naturally Small or Medium Zoan does not gain additional Reach simply by choosing this Talent.

Growing Ferocity

Passive

When your HP fall below half their maximum value, the instincts of the creature become more aggressive.

You gain +1 Damage Die to melee attacks until the end of the encounter.

The benefit activates when the HP condition is met.

It does not require a Bonus Action.

Being subsequently healed above half HP does not remove the benefit.

9.9.5 · d12: Mastery

Beast Resilience

Passive · Hybrid or Beast Form

The transformed body develops exceptional resistance to wounds.

Once per round, when you take damage from an attack, you can reduce by:

1 Grade

all the Damage Dice of that attack.

Progression:

d20 → d12
d12 → d10
d10 → d8
d8 → d6
d6 → d4

d4 is the minimum.

Beast Resilience does not use the Reaction.

The Talent does not remove Statuses, Displacements, conditions, other secondary effects of the attack or fixed damage not expressed through Damage Dice.

Ancient Heritage

Passive

Requirement: Ancient Zoan.

The character reaches sufficient mastery to exploit an exceptional characteristic of their ancient creature.

The Zoan was already Ancient from the creation of the Fruit: the Talent does not change the category; it unlocks an advanced ability connected to the ancient nature of the creature.

This can be:

  • An exceptional body structure.
  • An unusual Natural Weapon.
  • A unique physical configuration.
  • An extraordinary biological ability.
  • An exceptional size.
  • Another characteristic of the species.

The potential characteristic is recorded on the Creature Sheet from the creation of the Fruit: the Talent represents the moment when the character learns to exploit it fully.

Mythical Heritage

Passive

Requirement: Mythical Zoan.

The character reaches sufficient mastery to manifest one of the supernatural abilities of their mythical creature.

The Zoan was already Mythical from the creation of the Fruit: the Talent does not change the category; it unlocks a supernatural ability not previously mastered.

This can be:

  • An element.
  • A supernatural phenomenon.
  • A regenerative ability.
  • A mystical manifestation.
  • A property exclusive to the creature.

The ability must be consistent with the Heritage indicated on the Creature Sheet.

An ability unlocked through Ancient or Mythical Heritage can become a narrative Source for new Fruit Techniques, following the normal Builder rules: the Talent does not automatically grant Builder effects, but the ability that allows them to be represented.

Survival Instinct

1 time per battle

When you would fall to 0 HP

When an attack or another effect would bring you to 0 HP, animal instinct allows you to remain standing.

You remain conscious with 1 HP until the end of your next turn.

The HP gained is temporary and does not represent healing: at the end of your next turn you fall to 0 HP, unless in the meantime you have received genuine healing sufficient to keep you above 0.

Instinctive Transformation

Reaction

Once per scene

0 ST

When you are about to be directly affected by an attack, a hostile Technique or another dangerous effect, you can use your Reaction to transform immediately.

The transformation occurs before the effect is resolved.

You can use Instinctive Transformation even when you are:

  • asleep;
  • Stunned;
  • unconscious.

The ability performs only the change of form.

It does not automatically activate other active Talents associated with the form taken.

Transformation economy

  • Normal transformation: main Action, 0 ST.
  • Three Forms: Bonus Action, 0 ST, once per turn.
  • Instinctive Transformation: Reaction, 0 ST, once per scene.

Call of the Blood

Passive · Narrative

Animals of your own species, or species close enough to recognise you, consider you a pack leader.

They do not attack you first and normally allow you to cross their territory.

With time and patience, they may come to follow you.

The Talent does not automatically grant mind control over animals or turn them into subordinate combatants without further narrative conditions.

Animals do not automatically become servants, combatants, permanent companions or creatures willing to sacrifice themselves. Risky requests or requests contrary to their natural behaviour may require time, narrative conditions or appropriate checks.

9.9.6 · d20: Awakening

Awakening · Zoan

Ultimate

Requirements: Fruit Die d20, Ambition and permission from the Game Master.

The character reaches:

Ultimate Form.

The body, instincts and fundamental characteristics of the Zoan reach a manifestation impossible through normal transformation.

Awakening lasts:

1 scene.

Awakened Regeneration

At the start of each turn you recover:

1d10 HP.

You cannot exceed your maximum HP.

Awakened Natural Weapons

Attacks made directly through the transformed body or Natural Weapons gain:

+2 Damage Dice.

The benefit grants no additional attacks.

Absolute Instinct

While in Ultimate Form you cannot be:

Stunned or Off-Balance.

Dominant Characteristic

Every Zoan Awakening also takes one fundamental characteristic of the creature to its extreme.

The Benefit is defined through the normal Awakening Guideline.

It may concern:

  • Movement
  • Defence
  • Size
  • Senses
  • Body control
  • Ancient or Mythical Heritage

There is no universal increase to Strength or Movement.

The Dominant Characteristic must reflect the specific nature of the creature and remains unchanged once defined.

The price of Awakening

At the end of the scene:

  • Stamina falls to 0;
  • the character cannot use Zoan transformations again until their next Long Rest.

Voluntarily ending Awakening before the end of the scene does not avoid these consequences.

Chapter 10 ·

Weapons and Equipment

A sword may be only a piece of iron. Or it may be a blade handed down through generations, forged by a master and capable of accompanying its owner to the end of the Grand Line.

In Grand Line Chronicles, weapons have a mechanical identity distinct from Techniques. The character learns to fight through their Style, develops moves through the Technique Builder and uses weapons as tools to execute them.

A weapon does not generate a Technique: it executes it.

Its Grade indicates the quality of the tool. Any special effect instead represents an additional property, distinct from Grade and subject to Game Master approval.

10.1 · The structure of a weapon

Every weapon is defined through the Weapon Builder.

Weapon sheet

CharacteristicFunction
NameIdentifies the weapon.
TypeDetermines the category and compatible Style.
GradeRepresents the quality of the weapon.
AttributeStrength or Technique.
ReachDetermines the distance at which it can be used.
Special effectAn additional property, if any.
ConditionsIndicate when the effect can be applied.
PriceAny cost of the special effect.
GM approvalConfirms that the properties have been agreed at the table.

Ordinary weapons do not necessarily need a special effect.

A d8 katana without effects remains a perfectly usable d8 katana, provided the character meets the required conditions.

Grade and special effect are two independent characteristics: increasing Grade does not automatically generate a new effect.

10.2 · The six weapon types

The system distinguishes six categories.

Blade

Katanas, swords, sabres and daggers.

Swordsman

Blunt

Maces, hammers, clubs and knuckle dusters.

Crusher

Polearm

Spears, staves, halberds and tridents.

Variable

Ranged

Pistols, rifles, bows and crossbows.

Sniper

Thrown

Knives, javelins, throwing axes and shuriken.

Shared access

Special

Whips, chains and unconventional tools.

To be checked

Style assignment determines which weapons can be used to execute the corresponding Techniques and, where allowed, exploit their special effects.

Special-type weapons belong to the original structure of the arsenal, but the Special Style is not currently selectable on the V2 sheet. Their presence in the catalogue therefore does not imply automatic compatibility with the available Styles.

10.3 · Polearms

Polearms have a special feature: they must be classified according to how they are used.

The Builder provides two modes.

Edged

Swordsman

Uses a point or blade as its main element.

Blunt

Crusher

Uses the shaft as an impact weapon.

The mode must be chosen when the weapon is built.

Example: a spear classified as Edged requires the Swordsman Style to exploit its special effect. An iron-shod staff classified as Blunt instead requires Crusher.

The mode cannot be left undefined when weapon compatibility must be established.

10.4 · Weapon Grade

Grade represents the quality of the weapon and is expressed through a die.

GradeQuality
d4Basic
d6Competent
d8Expert
d10Master
d12Legend
d20Mythic

Weapon Grade is distinct from Technique Grade and the Racial Die.

A character could have a d10 katana and a d8 Technique: the two values do not automatically become identical.

10.4.1 · Attribute requirement

To use a weapon, the die of its reference Attribute must equal or exceed the Weapon Grade.

Attribute Die ≥ Weapon Grade

A character with d8 Technique can use a weapon associated with d8 Technique, but does not meet the requirement for a d10 weapon.

The rule is particularly relevant when a pirate obtains an extraordinary weapon before developing their abilities sufficiently to wield it effectively.

Striker exception: a Striker fights bare-handed and needs no Weapon Grade for their Techniques. They use their Attribute without being limited by the requirement of a wielded weapon.

The Builder records Attribute and Grade as separate fields. The requirement must be checked at the table: merely having the weapon on the sheet does not prove that the character automatically meets every condition.

10.5 · The weapon Attribute

Every weapon uses one of the two Attributes provided by the Builder.

AttributeApplication
StrengthWeapons used primarily through power and physical control.
TechniqueWeapons used primarily through precision and skill.

The Attribute is chosen during construction and agreed with the GM.

It is not assigned automatically by the name of the weapon.

Two weapons of the same Type can use different Attributes when their design and the way they are wielded justify it.

For example, a particularly heavy sword could be associated with Strength, while a blade designed for precise blows could use Technique.

The GM must approve the association, preventing the Attribute from being freely changed for each attack.

10.6 · Weapons and combat

A weapon can be used to make a basic attack or as a tool to execute a compatible Technique.

The two situations use different formulas.

10.6.1 · Basic attack

When a character makes a basic attack with a weapon, roll:

Attack roll

Attribute + Weapon Grade

The result is compared with the target's Defence.

The Damage Die of the basic attack follows Weapon Grade, unless effects specify otherwise.

Example

A pirate with d8 Strength uses a d8 mace.

Their attack roll is:

1d8 (Strength) + 1d8 (Weapon)

If they hit, they roll weapon damage, applying only any permitted modifiers.

10.6.2 · Technique attack

When the character uses a Technique, the attack roll follows this formula:

Attribute + Technique Die

The Damage Die is that of the Technique.

Weapon Grade does not replace the Technique Die.

A d12 sword does not automatically turn a d8 Technique into a d12 Technique.

The weapon allows the move to be executed and can contribute through a compatible special effect of its own, but does not grant a free increase in Grade.

10.7 · Reach

Reach indicates the distance at which a weapon can reach its target.

Melee weapons in the initial catalogue use the general description Melee.

Actual reach can vary according to the characteristics of the weapon, the character's Race and any specific abilities.

For ranged, thrown and special weapons, the V2 catalogue does not yet assign a universal value in metres.

The value must be recorded on the sheet when it is defined by the GM.

Weapon typeInitial indication
BladeMelee
BluntMelee
PolearmMelee, with actual reach to be defined
RangedReach to be recorded
ThrownReach to be recorded
SpecialReach to be recorded

It is not correct to automatically assign 20 metres to every pistol or 10 metres to every throwing knife. The value depends on the weapon actually designed.

When a Technique specifies its own reach, the rules of that Technique also apply.

10.8 · Style compatibility

The system distinguishes three aspects: owning a weapon, being able to use it and being able to exploit its special effect.

They are not necessarily the same thing.

10.8.1 · Style determines the arsenal

Weapons are assigned to Combat Styles.

StyleAssociated weapons
SwordsmanBlade, Edged polearm
CrusherBlunt, Blunt polearm
SniperRanged, thrown
StrikerBare hands for Style Techniques

Thrown weapons are a shared category for Arsenal places.

However, their compatibility with special effects and Style Techniques follows the association specified for Sniper.

Example

A Crusher can have access to a thrown weapon in their Arsenal.

This does not turn them into a Sniper or allow them to execute Sniper Techniques.

To exploit a special effect that expressly requires the Sniper Style, they must possess that Style.

10.8.2 · Incompatible weapons

When the character does not possess the Style required by the weapon, they cannot use its special effect.

V2 management distinguishes this case from using the simple Weapon Grade.

Style and Arsenal limitations remain in force: a weapon recorded on the sheet does not automatically become equippable.

10.8.3 · Characters without the Fighter Role

In the V2 management system, Arsenal places depend on the Fighter Role and the Styles possessed.

A character without the Fighter Role does not have the corresponding weapon places.

They can possess items in their inventory and use the tools of their profession, but do not automatically gain access to Style Techniques or weapon special effects.

10.9 · Arsenal places

Each Combat Style normally grants one place for a compatible weapon.

A character who possesses two Styles can have one place for each.

Compatibility is checked across all the weapons present: it is not enough for each weapon to be generically compatible; there must be enough places for all of them.

ConfigurationAvailable places
One Style1 weapon
Two Styles1 weapon per Style
Swordsman with More Swords · BasicAbility to carry 2 blades
Swordsman with two Styles in totalPlaces determined by the combination of Styles and Talents

10.9.1 · More Swords

The More Swords · Basic Talent allows the Swordsman a second place for a blade.

It does not grant a generic place for a weapon of any Type.

The second place is therefore reserved for blades.

Example

A Swordsman possesses More Swords · Basic.

They can record two katanas in their dedicated places.

They cannot freely replace the second katana with a rifle, because the Talent does not grant compatibility with the Sniper Style.

10.10 · Weapon special effects

A weapon can have at most one special effect.

The effect is defined separately from Grade and agreed with the Game Master.

A d4 weapon can possess an unusual effect if the story and GM allow it. Conversely, a d20 weapon does not necessarily receive an effect merely because it is Mythic.

The Builder divides effects into five categories.

Discount

Reduces a cost or the number of slots required by a specific effect.

Unlock

Allows a specific requirement to be bypassed within the limits established by the effect.

Passive

Grants a benefit when the weapon conditions are met.

Reaction

Allows intervention in response to a specific event.

Curse

Offers power accompanied by a price or a negative consequence.

Effects must be worded precisely.

A description such as “the sword is extremely powerful” is not a sufficient rule.

You must specify what benefit it provides, when it applies, how much it costs and what limits it has.

10.10.1 · Discount

A Discount effect must indicate exactly:

The cost being reduced.

The effect or ability concerned.

The amount of the discount.

The conditions for applying it.

Any remaining minimum cost.

A Discount does not automatically reduce all of the character’s costs.

Example of a custom effect: a particular katana could reduce the cost of a specific Technique by 1 ST, to a minimum of 1 ST, once per encounter.

These values are a design example, not a benefit assigned to all katanas.

10.10.2 · Unlock

An Unlock effect allows an exception to a defined requirement.

It can make a Builder effect accessible early, when the GM approves.

The description must indicate which requirement is modified.

Unlocking an effect does not automatically remove all other requirements.

If a weapon allows an effect normally available at d10 to be used at d8, the character must still respect the other constraints of the Technique, such as Style compatibility, Source and slot availability, unless exceptions are expressly specified.

10.10.3 · Passive

A Passive produces a benefit without requiring voluntary activation each time.

The benefit is available only when the weapon effect can actually be used.

For example, a weapon could grant a specific bonus while wielded or when a condition is met.

The effect does not automatically transfer to all the character’s other weapons or Techniques.

10.10.4 · Reaction

A Reaction effect must specify:

When: the event that allows it to be used.

Result: what happens when it is activated.

Frequency: how many times it can be used.

Price: any cost required.

If the ability requires a Reaction, the character must have their Reaction available.

A Reaction effect does not automatically grant an additional Reaction.

10.10.5 · Curse

A Curse associates power with a price.

The description must specify both.

The price can involve Stamina, HP, charges or a particular consequence defined by the GM.

There is no universal penalty assigned to all cursed weapons.

A cursed blade could require a sacrifice to activate, while another could impose particular conditions on its user.

The two weapons must be built separately.

10.11 · Effect frequency and price

Every special effect must state its frequency.

FrequencyMeaning
AlwaysThe benefit applies whenever the conditions are met.
1×/turnCan be used once per turn.
1×/combatCan be used once per combat.
1×/sceneCan be used once per scene.
Charge-basedConsumes a limited resource.

The effect can also specify one of the following prices.

PriceHow it works
NoneRequires no additional resources.
StaminaConsumes the indicated amount of ST.
HPRequires the sacrifice of the specified HP.
One chargeConsumes one weapon charge.
OtherUses an agreed resource or condition.

Simply choosing the category does not determine the numerical value of the cost.

If an effect has a price in HP or Stamina, the amount must be recorded.

10.12 · Ordinary and special weapons

The Builder distinguishes weapons without a special effect from those with one.

Ordinary weapon

Standard katana

Grade d4

Type: Blade

Reach: Melee

Effect: none

Custom weapon

Twilight Katana

Grade d8

Type: Blade

Reach: Melee

Effect: to be agreed with the GM.

The second weapon does not automatically gain an effect because it has a higher Grade.

The player must design it and the Game Master must approve it (unless the Game Master creates the weapons and their corresponding effects for their own session).

Approval is noted on the weapon sheet. The tick records the agreement; it is not automatic validation of the rules.

10.13 · Ammunition and reloading

Ranged weapons use compatible ammunition: bullets, arrows, bolts or other types specified by their construction.

Ammunition and reloading are two distinct elements. Having ammunition does not mean the weapon is already loaded; reloading, conversely, does not create new ammunition.

The weapon sheet must indicate its capacity and any reload conditions. The V2 catalogue does not establish a universal capacity for all weapons: these details must be defined for the specific model, without arbitrarily assigning the same number of shots to pistols, rifles and bows.

Ordinary reloading

Reloading a weapon normally requires an Action when its sheet specifies a reload.

Weapons that can fire several shots before being reloaded follow their own capacity.

Sniper Talent · Rapid Reload

Rapid Reload

d8

Once per turn, the Sniper can reload a weapon without consuming their main Action.

In the V2 system, activating the Talent uses the only Bonus Action of the turn.

The Sniper can therefore reload and attack in the same turn, but cannot also use that Bonus Action to activate Haki or another active Talent.

Special ammunition

Special ammunition consists of consumables with their own properties, such as modified bullets, bolts or other preparations.

To use one, the character must possess it and the weapon must be compatible. Its effect applies according to the ammunition description.

Special ammunition can be built through the normal Workshop rules when the character has the corresponding blueprint, necessary materials and appropriate Skill.

Chapter 11 ·

The Workshop and Crafting

A pirate ship does not survive through its fighters alone. When a cannon shot destroys the rudder, a weapon jams or a new contraption could turn the tide of a boarding action, it takes capable hands to turn materials and blueprints into something concrete.

The Workshop allows objects to be built, modified and repaired. Crafting is the system used to resolve these activities through Skills, materials, time and Checks.

You do not need to belong to a particular Role to attempt a construction. What matters is having the expertise required by the blueprint and the conditions needed to realise it.

This chapter presents the general construction rules. Particular objects and special blueprints can be introduced by the Game Master during the campaign.

11.1 · What is a blueprint?

A blueprint describes an object the character intends to build or work they want to carry out on something that already exists.

It may concern a simple tool, special ammunition, a mechanical weapon or a device intended for the ship.

Before starting, the characteristics of the blueprint must be defined.

Blueprint sheet

ElementDescription
NameIdentifies the object to build.
FunctionDescribes what it must actually do.
SkillDetermines the expertise required.
ThresholdEstablishes the difficulty of construction.
TempoIndicates the working hours required.
MaterialsLists required ingredients and components.
EffectDescribes any mechanical benefit.
LimitationsSpecifies uses, maintenance and other conditions.

A blueprint can belong to the equipment normally available or be a special blueprint introduced by the Game Master.

In both cases, Function, Skill, Threshold, Time, Materials, any Effect and Limitations must be established before the construction Check.

A high result does not allow abilities not included in the blueprint to be added to the object.

11.2 · Who can build?

Anyone with the appropriate Skill can attempt a blueprint within their expertise.

The Skill depends on the object and the activity.

SkillApplications
CraftingTools, manual work and ordinary objects.
MechanicsContraptions, engines, mechanical weapons and devices.
CarpentryWooden structures, hull and ship components.
MedicineMedical tools and preparations, where appropriate.
NavigationNautical charts and instruments requiring knowledge of courses.

The blueprint sheet determines the exact Check. When designing and assembling a contraption, a common formula is:

Construction Check

Cunning + Mechanics

Or the Skill specified by the blueprint.

Knowing a Skill does not automatically grant the Talents of Roles that use that same expertise.

A pirate with d8 Mechanics can, for example, build or repair a device compatible with their expertise. However, they do not possess Rapid Response or Hands in Oil unless they have acquired those Talents.

The role of the Engineer

The Engineer has two specialisations.

SpecialisationMain expertise
Carpenter · CarpentryHull, structures and naval repairs.
Mechanic · MechanicsMachines, cannons, engines and devices.

These specialisations grant their respective Talents, but do not determine who can use Crafting. A character with the appropriate Skill can attempt a blueprint within their expertise, respecting its Threshold, materials, time and requirements.

11.3 · The stages of Crafting

Construction follows a common procedure.

  • Define the blueprint
    The player describes what they want to achieve. The GM establishes whether the object can be made and what abilities it can possess.
  • Determine requirements
    Identify Skill, Threshold, materials, tools and working hours.
  • Obtain materials
    The components must be available. Some can be purchased; others require exploration or salvage.
  • Carry out the work
    The character devotes the established hours to the blueprint, using the required tools and conditions.
  • Make the Check
    The result determines whether the blueprint succeeds and the quality achieved.
  • Record the object
    The completed object is noted in the inventory or appropriate sheet, together with its effects and limitations.

The blueprint must be defined before the roll. You cannot wait for a particularly high result to declare that the object has abilities never agreed upon.

11.4 · Establishing the Threshold

Every blueprint requires a Threshold.

The GM determines it by considering the complexity of the object, the precision needed, working conditions and required knowledge.

Use the general scale for Checks.

ThresholdDifficulty
4Trivial
8Normal
12Expert
16Vice-Admiral
20Admiral
25+Legend

Object sheets can state specific Thresholds that do not exactly match these reference values.

The Threshold stated by the blueprint takes precedence over generic difficulty.

An object requiring Threshold 11 keeps Threshold 11: it must not automatically be rounded to 12.

Unfavourable conditions

Working without appropriate tools, on a ship in a storm or with components of insufficient quality can make the Check harder.

The GM applies appropriate changes according to the normal rules for Checks.

There is no universal penalty for every missing tool. Some jobs may simply be impossible without the necessary equipment.

11.5 · Construction time

Time is expressed in working hours.

Every blueprint indicates how much time it requires under ordinary conditions.

The character can split the work across several days, tracking the hours completed.

If a blueprint requires 20 hours and the character completes 8, 12 working hours remain.

Hours spent working do not automatically count as hours of rest.

Working during a voyage

The onboard Workshop allows the crew to use voyages to build and repair objects, provided they have the necessary tools and materials.

The GM can interrupt the work when conditions do not allow it.

A workbench may be usable during calm sailing. It could instead become unusable while the ship faces a storm or takes part in a battle.

Several characters on the same blueprint

Several characters can collaborate when the work allows it.

The GM establishes how to divide the activities.

Having two workers does not automatically halve every construction time. A process that requires waiting for metal to cool, for example, does not become instant by increasing the number of people.

Specific Talents can instead explicitly modify the hours required.

11.6 · Resolving the Check

At the end of the required work, the character makes the Check specified by the blueprint.

Margin = result − Threshold

The result determines whether construction succeeds.

A blueprint completed with a high margin can produce an object of superior quality.

In particular, the system distinguishes a Masterpiece, which allows a workmanship advantage to be chosen.

MarginOutcome
NegativeThe Check fails.
0Barely succeeds, with any complications established by the GM.
+1 / +3Successful.
+4 or higherMasterpiece.

The specific consequence of failure depends on the blueprint. The current rules do not specify that every failure automatically destroys all materials.

The GM determines whether the character must correct the work, obtain new components or restart a stage of construction.

The Masterpiece

A Masterpiece allows one advantage to be chosen from those specified for the work.

AdvantageResult
TempoThe hours required are halved.
MaterialsMaterial consumption is halved.
FinishingThe object receives a superior finish appropriate to the blueprint.

The finish must be defined with the GM.

It does not automatically grant a new Damage Die, an Effect slot or an ability not specified by the blueprint.

A Masterpiece improves workmanship quality; it does not introduce new functions or effects into the object.

If you choose the Time advantage after the Check, subtract half the specified hours from the total and make them available again for other activities. For example, a 16-hour blueprint consumes 8 and leaves the other 8 free. The benefit also applies to hours already counted: crafting time is managed as an available resource, without requiring a detailed chronology.

11.7 · Materials

A blueprint requires the materials indicated on its sheet.

The character must obtain them before completing construction.

Materials can be purchased, gathered, extracted from existing objects or obtained through adventure.

Material availability

SourceExample situation
PurchaseBuying iron in a port.
GatheringSearching for timber on an island.
SalvageDismantling abandoned machinery.
RewardObtaining a component from an artisan.
Narrative researchDiscovering where a unique material can be found.

Not all materials can be purchased.

Seastone, certain abyssal ingredients and other extremely rare components may require a dedicated mission.

Narrative research represents the time needed to discover information, find a place or locate a supplier. It is not a physical material to add to the inventory.

Salvaging components

Dismantling an object or exploring a wreck can allow usable parts to be salvaged.

The GM establishes the amount and condition of the materials available.

Talents that improve salvage, such as the Carpenter's Lumberjack and the Mechanic's Dismantler, apply their benefits to the corresponding Checks.

Dismantling an object does not automatically return 100% of the materials used to build it.

11.8 · The Merchant and prices

Material prices are expressed in Berry.

The system's economy distinguishes five tiers, useful for establishing how accessible a component is.

TierIndicative priceAvailability
CommonUp to 1,000 ฿Villages and ordinary settlements
Standard1,001–5,000 ฿Ports
Good5,001–15,000 ฿Trading ports
Fine15,001–50,000 ฿Large ports and orders
RareOver 50,000 ฿Special suppliers or the black market

These tiers are commercial reference points, not the automatic price of any constructed object.

Material sheets state the specific cost per unit or batch.

Negotiations and market conditions

Good negotiation based on Communication can obtain a 10–20% discount when the merchant is willing to negotiate.

On an isolated island, during a naval blockade or in wartime, the price can double.

A pirate with a high bounty may also face a 25% surcharge or encounter merchants who refuse to serve them.

These conditions do not automatically apply to every purchase: they depend on the location and situation.

The digital Merchant catalogue contains the prices of individual ingredients. The manual uses only the general rules of trade.

11.9 · The four families of craftable objects

The Workshop includes numerous types of blueprints. Four families are particularly relevant to combat and exploration.

FamilyFunction
AmmunitionConsumables intended for weapons and combat.
Field gadgetsEquipment for exploration, mobility and utility.
Mechanical weaponsWeapons that incorporate devices or mechanisms.
Experimental modulesDevices that can be installed according to the Module rules.

The families identify the type of blueprint. They do not automatically grant bonuses.

A grappling hook, for example, allows the physical functions specified by its blueprint to be performed. It does not provide a bonus to Checks merely because it belongs to the Gadget family.

Likewise, a multi-barrelled pistol must specify capacity, conditions and operation on its sheet.

11.10 · Consumable objects

A consumable object is used a limited number of times.

Its sheet must state the conditions that determine when it is depleted.

A bomb is normally consumed when used. A kit may instead allow several uses before requiring replenishment.

The available quantity must be recorded in the inventory.

Special ammunition

Special ammunition follows the rules of Chapter 10.

The Workshop allows it to be built, while the weapon determines whether it can be used.

Each special ammunition type has its own blueprint, establishing Skill, Threshold, Time, Materials, quantity produced and any Effect.

Particular effects, damage modifications or Statuses are not freely obtained during construction: they must already be specified by the ammunition blueprint.

11.11 · Modifying an object

A character can work on something they already possess.

The work may consist of a repair, replacing a component or a modification specified by a particular blueprint.

The GM treats the modification as a normal Crafting blueprint, establishing Skill, Threshold, Time, Materials and result.

A modification does not automatically increase Weapon Grade or freely add Special Effects.

Weapon Grade represents its quality and continues to follow the normal rules of Chapter 10.

If a modification is to grant a new mechanical benefit, that benefit must be explicitly specified by the blueprint used.

11.12 · Repairing objects

Repairing means restoring a damaged object to a usable state.

Repair requires an appropriate Skill, materials and time.

Machinery is normally handled through Mechanics. A naval structure requires Carpentry.

The simple ability to build something does not remove the requirements of the work.

Malfunction and Destruction

Machinery can be in two different conditions.

Malfunctioning. The device is still structurally intact, but part of its operation is blocked, jammed or out of order. A malfunction can be removed through a normal repair or an ability that expressly permits immediate intervention. Destroyed. The device has suffered serious structural damage, lost essential components or is no longer usable in its current form. Rebuilding it normally requires time, materials and the Workshop rules.

An ability that allows a Malfunction to be removed does not automatically allow a Destroyed device to be rebuilt.

A machinery sheet or specific ability may establish different conditions.

Repairs outside combat

The GM establishes the Threshold and hours required according to the damage.

A lightly damaged object may require brief work. A destroyed one may instead need complete reconstruction and new materials.

There is no universal percentage of hours or materials applicable to every repair.

Repairs during combat

A normal Crafting activity does not automatically become executable in an Action.

Immediate repairs require abilities that expressly allow them.

Examples of repair Talents

Emergency Repair · Carpenter

During a naval battle, you use the Action to repair the hull. A successful Check immediately restores SP to the ship.

Rapid Response · Mechanic

Allows a contraption, cannon or malfunctioning machine to be quickly restored to operation according to the conditions of the Talent.

Hands in Oil · Mechanic

With a Workshop available, you halve repair times. Your tools do not wear out.

When a Talent specifies that the work requires an Action, the V2 Bonus Action rule does not automatically change how it works.

11.13 · The quality of Engineers’ work

Engineers possess abilities that directly modify certain jobs linked to their specialisation.

These benefits are not universal Workshop rules: they belong to characters who have acquired the corresponding abilities.

Carpenter · A Repair That Holds

When the Carpenter achieves a +4 margin on a Carpentry Check to repair the ship, the repair restores twice the specified SP.

The benefit concerns hull repair, not any object built by the Carpenter.

Mechanic · Running Like a Dream

When the Mechanic achieves a +4 margin on a Mechanics Check while operating a machine, that machine acts twice in the turn.

The Signature concerns machinery operation; it does not automatically double the quality of every constructed object.

11.14 · Special blueprints

Not all objects craftable on the Grand Line belong to ordinary equipment.

During the campaign, the Game Master can introduce special blueprints: unusual weapons, ammunition, tools, devices, ship improvements or other technologies that characters can build.

A special blueprint uses the same Blueprint Sheet as any other construction:

Name · Function · Skill · Threshold · Time · Materials · Effect · Limitations

The blueprint establishes in advance what the object can do.

Having the blueprint does not mean automatically having the object. To realise it, you must meet its requirements, obtain materials, devote the necessary time and make the specified Check.

A character can attempt construction if they have the required Skill. They do not need to belong to a specific Role unless the blueprint itself states a particular requirement.

Special Effects and other object abilities are established by the blueprint. They cannot be freely added following a particularly high roll.

The GM can use special blueprints as campaign rewards, discoveries or opportunities and may require rare components or particular conditions when the technology justifies it.

Once the blueprint is obtained, construction normally follows all the Workshop rules.

11.15 · Module construction and installation

Modules use the normal Workshop rules, supplemented by the specific rules in the Cyborg Manual.

Technology Tier determines the minimum Mechanics expertise, Threshold and base time required for construction.

A completed Module can exist without being installed. In that case, it is stored as a normal object until the Cyborg has a free slot and sufficient Mechanical Body.

Installing a Module requires:

  • a free slot;
  • Mechanical Body at least equal to the Module Tier;
  • the completed device;
  • appropriate tools.
TierBase installation time
d630 minutes
d81 hour
d102 hours
d124 hours
d208 hours

Replacing a Module uses the time for the new installation and includes removing the previous one.

Upgrading a Module to a higher Tier constitutes a new blueprint.

11.16 · Complete Crafting example

An Engineer wants to build a handmade Log Pose for the crew.

The blueprint sheet specifies Cunning + Mechanics, Threshold 11, 20 working hours and specific materials: refined magnetic iron, a sensitive needle, blown glass and leather.

Blueprint · Handmade Log Pose

  • Threshold 11
  • 20 hours
  • Cunning + Mechanics
  • Preparation

The character obtains all materials and has access to a suitable Workshop.

They complete the specified working hours.

Check

The dice result is 15.

15 − 11 = +4

The blueprint achieves a Masterpiece result.

Benefit

The player chooses the time advantage: of the 20 hours counted, the blueprint consumes only 10. The other 10 become available again and can be devoted to another blueprint or activity. It is a subtraction from the available working time: there is no need to reconstruct a more precise chronology.

The Log Pose is completed with the properties specified on its sheet.

Subsequent calibration still requires the time indicated by the blueprint: 2d6 days.

Construction quality does not automatically cancel the operating requirements of the object.

Likewise, achieving a Masterpiece does not turn the Log Pose into an Eternal Pose or give it a new permanent destination.

The blueprint produces what was agreed, with the workmanship benefit actually gained.

Chapter 12 ·

Mechanical Body and Modules

A steel arm can conceal a cannon. A mechanical leg can contain a thruster. Behind a simple metal plate, there could be a shield capable of saving a companion from a cannon shot.

On the Grand Line, technology is not merely a collection of tools: it can become part of a pirate’s body.

In Grand Line Chronicles, Modules are installable devices that provide physical functions and, where specified, a special effect. Their operation is governed by slots, technological requirements, activation and any energy resources.

A Module is not a Technique and is not necessarily a weapon.

Its purpose is to add a concrete ability to the character, usable independently or through compatible Techniques.

This chapter summarises the fundamental rules needed to use Modules during play. The complete Technology Tier tables, Module design, Special Effects, Energy, construction, installation and repair are explored in depth in the Cyborg Manual.

12.1 · What is a Module?

A Module is a mechanical device installed on the character’s body.

It can replace an anatomical part, be integrated into a limb or constitute an additional apparatus.

Every Module has a physical function describing what the device is able to do.

It may also have a special effect, provided it is designed and approved by the Game Master.

Anatomy of a Module

FieldContent
NameDevice identity
Technology Tierd6, d8, d10, d12 o d20
CategoryOffensive, Defensive, Mobility, Control or Utility
StructureFunctional or Weapon Module
Weapon GradeOnly for a Weapon Module
Physical functionWhat the device is built to do
Inactive functionWhat remains usable when Inactive
Active functionWhat it can do while operational
Special EffectOptional; at most one
EnergyResource type, where specified
Capacity / Consumption / RechargeResource management
Compatible TechniquesAny limits as an executor
LimitsSpecific blueprint conditions
IntegrityIntact or Damaged
InstallationInstalled or Not Installed
ActivationActive or Inactive

Physical function and special effect are distinct.

An extendable arm can stretch and grab objects because that is its function.

If the device must also reduce the cost of a Technique with Attraction, that discount must be recorded as a special effect.

Simply possessing the arm does not automatically grant both benefits.

12.2 · Who can use Modules?

The system provides two sources of access to Modules: the Cyborg Race and, where available, access through specific abilities.

12.2.1 · Cyborg Race

The Cyborg possesses the Mechanical Body Racial Technique.

This Racial Technique allows devices to be installed in their body, progressively increasing the number of available slots.

The ability depends on the Cyborg’s Racial Die.

The character can choose Modules compatible with their technological level, respecting blueprint requirements.

12.3 · Mechanical Body slots

Slots represent the body’s capacity to house devices.

Each installed Module takes up one slot.

The maximum number depends on Mechanical Body Grade.

Racial DieAvailable slots
d61
d82
d103
d124
d205

Slots are unlocked through Racial Die progression.

A Cyborg with d8 Mechanical Body therefore has two slots. When they reach d10, they increase to three.

12.3.1 · Initial Modules

Cyborg creation

2 Modules

At creation, the Cyborg receives two free Modules, with no initial purchase or construction costs.

The two Modules are agreed with the GM. Their installation must respect technological requirements and the slots actually available.

With d6 Mechanical Body, the Cyborg has only one slot: they can install one of the two initial Modules. When they reach d8, there are two slots and they can also install the second free Module. Possessing a Module does not mean it is already installed.

Subsequent installations normally follow the Workshop rules.

12.4 · Technology Tier

Every Module belongs to a Technology Tier.

Tier indicates the minimum Mechanical Body Grade needed to install it and establishes the maximum limit of its technological performance.

TierMechanical Body required
d6d6
d8d8
d10d10
d12d12
d20d20

Technology Tier and Weapon Grade are distinct values.

A Weapon Module can have a Weapon Grade equal to or below its Technology Tier, never above it.

Example: Module d10 · Weapon d8 is valid. Module d8 · Weapon d10 is not.

12.5 · The five Module functions

During construction, the player assigns the Module the Category or Categories that describe its Function.

Offensive

Devices intended to attack.

Defensive

Barriers, shields and mechanical protections.

Control

Tools that grab, restrain or manipulate.

Mobility

Thrusters, cables and displacement systems.

Utility

Tools and practical functions that are not necessarily combat-related.

A Module can have more than one Category.

An extendable arm, for example, could belong to both Control and Mobility.

Categories classify the function of the device: they do not grant automatic bonuses.

An Offensive Module does not deal damage merely because it is classified as Offensive. To attack, it must have a suitable structure and respect the weapon or Technique rules.

12.6 · Functional Modules and Weapon Modules

Every Module must be classified as functional or usable as a weapon.

12.6.1 · Functional Modules

A functional Module performs a task without possessing a Weapon Grade.

It may be a pincer, a tool compartment or a device that lights the environment.

Functional Module

Mechanical pincer

Function: grab and manipulate objects.

Weapon Grade: none.

Special effect: optional.

Requirement: as indicated by the blueprint.

Its function can be used when the device is intact and the situation allows it.

The pincer can grab something because it was built to do so. However, it does not automatically grant a bonus to grapples or the Attraction effect.

A further mechanical benefit requires an ability that specifies it.

12.6.2 · Weapon Modules

A Weapon Module is a device usable to make attacks.

It possesses a Grade and follows the general weapon rules.

GradeQuality
d4Basic
d6Competent
d8Expert
d10Master
d12Legend
d20Mythic

The Grade of the Weapon Module is not automatically determined by its technological requirement.

Example: an integrated cannon could require d10 Mechanical Body but have Grade d8.

When used for a basic attack, it follows the weapon attack rules.

When executing a linked Technique, it uses the Technique Die; it does not automatically replace that die with its own Grade.

12.7 · Activating Modules

A Cyborg can have several installed Modules, but normally keeps only one Active Module at a time.

The Active Module can use its active Function, Special Effect and linked Techniques.

The other installed Modules remain Inactive and retain only the inactive Functions stated on their sheets.

Initial Module

Outside combat, the Cyborg freely chooses which Module to keep Active.

At the start of an encounter, they keep the one already Active. If none was declared, they choose one without spending Actions.

Changing Module

Once during their turn, the Cyborg can change the Active Module for free.

The change consumes no Action, Bonus Action, Movement or Reaction.

The new Module becomes Active immediately and the previous one becomes Inactive.

A second free change cannot be made in the same turn.

Outside your turn

Normally, a Module cannot be changed outside your turn. A specific ability may allow it through a Reaction.

12.8 · The physical functions of inactive Modules

An Inactive Module retains only the inactive Function recorded on its sheet.

The inactive Function includes physical properties that do not require the system to be operational: for example, a compartment that continues to hold objects or an artificial limb that retains normal mobility.

Propulsion, advanced sensors, defensive benefits, Special Effects and linked Techniques do not automatically remain available.

If the sheet states no inactive Function, the Inactive Module grants no mechanical benefits.

12.9 · Special effects

A Module can possess at most one Special Effect. The Effect is optional and grants only one main mechanical benefit.

The available categories are:

Discount · Unlock · Passive · Reaction · Curse

Technology Tier determines the maximum intensity of the Effect according to the Cyborg Manual tables.

A Special Effect cannot conceal several independent benefits and does not automatically grant additional Actions, additional Reactions, Talents, Haki, Devil Fruits, Ultimates, Prestige or infinite resources.

Unless explicit exceptions apply, the Effect requires the Module to be Installed, Intact and Active.

12.10 · Energy and charges

Not all Modules require a separate resource.

Every sheet indicates Energy: None or a consistent source, such as a battery, fuel, pressure, ammunition or other Charges.

When a Module uses Charges, its ordinary capacity depends on Tier:

TierMaximum Charges
d63
d84
d105
d126
d208

A normal use consumes 1 Charge unless the blueprint states otherwise.

Charges and Stamina are different resources. If a Technique costs ST and the Module executing it consumes a Charge, both are paid.

Charges are not automatically recovered through Rest.

If the necessary resource is available, a normal full recharge requires 10 minutes outside combat.

12.11 · Using Modules in combat

A Module can be used through its physical function, as a weapon or as a Technique executor.

These possibilities remain distinct.

UseRule
Physical functionFollows the device description.
Basic attackRequires a usable Weapon Module.
Linked TechniqueRequires the Module to be active and intact.
Special effectRequires the conditions recorded on the Module.
ReactionRequires the Reaction, where specified.

12.11.1 · Modules and Techniques

A Technique can be linked to a compatible Module through the Builder.

The link indicates which device is used to execute that move.

A Technique can use a weapon or a Module as its executor, never both simultaneously.

The Module does not generate the Technique.

The Technique must be possessed by the character and respect Grade, Attribute, Form, effects and costs.

If the Module is inactive, it must be activated before executing the Technique.

If it is damaged, the linked Technique cannot be executed until the device is repaired.

12.11.2 · Example of use

Example · Mechanical Harpoon

At the start of their turn, the Cyborg has another Active Module.

They change the Active Module for free, choosing the Mechanical Harpoon.

In the same turn, they can use their Action to make an attack or execute a compatible Technique through the Harpoon.

If the Technique costs ST and the Harpoon consumes Charges, they pay both resources.

Changing Module does not use the Bonus Action, which therefore remains available for Haki or Talents.

12.12 · Modules and Special Moves

A Special Move can use a Module as the executor of its Technique.

The device must already be active and intact when the Special Move is executed.

Any special effect of the Module applies only when its conditions are met.

The Special Move does not grant free activations, additional charges or discounts not specified.

12.12.1 · Preparation over several turns

A Special Move that requires Haki and an active Talent may need several turns of preparation because the two share the Bonus Action. The Module does not cause this preparation: it becomes Active through the turn’s free change.

Example · Reactor Strike

Sequence with Module, Haki and Talent

Turn 1

Preparing Haki

Bonus Action: activate Haki, paying the necessary PIP.

Turn 2

Executing the move

Maintain Haki, paying any ST cost.

Bonus Action: activate a compatible Talent.

Free change: make the Module Active, if it is not already.

Action: execute the Special Move using the Module.

Pay the Stamina of the Technique and any energy consumption of the Module.

The sequence is valid only when the Module has the necessary resources and all abilities involved are compatible.

The character cannot select a second Module as executor of the same Technique merely to add its effects.

12.13 · Damaged Modules

A Module has two integrity states.

Intact

Operational

Can be used according to its rules.

Damaged

Non-operational

Cannot use its function, effect or linked Techniques.

A damaged Module stops working until repaired.

If it was the active Module, it remains recorded as the selected device but is marked as non-operational.

The character can choose to change Module according to the normal activation rules.

12.13.1 · When is it damaged?

Damage is determined by the Game Master as a narrative consequence of an attack, a particular situation or an overload specified by the blueprint.

Modules have no automatic Hit Point threshold.

A character does not automatically lose a Module whenever they suffer a certain amount of damage.

Likewise, reaching half HP does not automatically damage all implants.

Effects that specifically target a device can instead produce appropriate consequences.

12.14 · Repairing a Module

A Damaged Module must be repaired before becoming operational again.

TierRepair time
d610 minutes
d820 minutes
d1030 minutes
d121 hour
d202 hours

With appropriate tools and spare parts, a normal repair outside combat succeeds without a Check.

If the work takes place in difficult conditions, with insufficient materials or on exceptional technology, the GM may require a Mechanics Check and establish the appropriate Threshold.

Repairing a Module does not restore HP to the Cyborg.

Recovering the body’s HP and repairing a Module’s Integrity are two distinct procedures.

12.15 · Installing a new Module

A completed blueprint is not necessarily an installed Module.

Installation follows the procedure specified by the Workshop.

Prepare the blueprint
Define the physical function, requirement and any special effect.

Check slots
The character must have a free slot.

Check compatibility
Mechanical Body must meet the technological requirement.

Obtain materials
Gather the necessary components.

Carry out the work
A competent character works with appropriate tools and conditions.

Record the Module
The device is added to Mechanical Body and becomes available for activation.

Installation requires a free slot, Mechanical Body at least equal to Tier, the completed device and appropriate tools.

Base time depends on Tier:

d6 → 30 minutes
d8 → 1 hour
d10 → 2 hours
d12 → 4 hours
d20 → 8 hours

Exceptional materials, particular conditions or non-standard technologies may change the procedure at the GM’s discretion.

The Cyborg’s two initial Modules are the exception to the normal acquisition procedure: they are granted free during creation.

12.16 · Experimental Modules

Experimental Modules are devices outside the ordinary options.

They can have original functions, but must respect the common structure: physical function, requirement, any Grade, only one Special Effect, resources and integrity.

The Game Master can introduce an experimental Module as a Special Blueprint. Once the blueprint is obtained, any character meeting the construction requirements can attempt to make it through the normal Workshop rules.

A ship Module is not automatically a body Module.

The two device types belong to different systems.

An engine installed in the engine room does not take up a Cyborg slot. A cannon integrated into an arm does not automatically take up a ship slot.

Installation rules and requirements must be applied to the correct system.

12.17 · Module operational sheet

The operational sheet must allow the device’s state to be consulted quickly during a session.

Module · Sheet

Name:

Technology Tier:

Category:

Structure:

Weapon Grade:

Physical function:

Inactive function:

Active function:

Special Effect:

Energy:

Capacity / Consumption / Recharge:

Compatible Techniques:

Limits:

Integrity: Intact / Damaged

Installation: Installed / Not Installed

Activation: Active / Inactive

The sheet must keep activation state and integrity state separate.

A device can be intact but inactive.

It can also remain selected as the active Module while damaged: in that case it does not work until it is repaired or replaced by another device.

Chapter 13 ·

The Ship and Navigation

Every crew needs a ship. It is the means of crossing the seas, but also the characters’ home, the place where their treasures are kept and, when cannons begin to thunder, the crew’s first line of defence.

In Grand Line Chronicles, the ship has its own sheet, statistics expressed through dice, Structure Points and installable Modules.

During a voyage, the Navigator guides it. In battle, instead, the whole crew must collaborate at the different stations.

A powerful ship without a capable crew remains only a large target.

13.1 · Creating the ship

Every ship has an identity, a technical sheet and a crew.

The player or Game Master must first define its narrative characteristics.

Ship sheet

ElementDescription
NameThe ship’s name.
TypeIts category and general structure.
FigureheadThe figure that characterises its bow.
WoodThe main material of the hull.
StatisticsHull, Sails, Artillery and Structure.
Structure PointsThe ship’s resilience.
ModulesInstalled equipment.
CrewCharacters assigned to stations.
StoreAvailable materials and resources.
LogbookCargo, Berry and voyage notes.

The name, figurehead and appearance do not automatically grant bonuses.

A figurehead may, however, become important to the story or gain specific properties through a Module.

The ship type is described freely and approved by the GM. There is no table automatically assigning different statistics to every vessel model.

13.2 · The four statistics

The ship has four fundamental statistics.

Hull

Resilience and Structure Points.

Sails

Navigation, speed, manoeuvres and initiative.

Artillery

Cannon power and naval attacks.

Structure

Capacity to install Modules and support technical work.

Naval statistics use a different progression from that of characters.

d4

d6

d8

d10

d12

The ordinary maximum Grade for naval statistics is d12.

Unlike personal statistics, ship statistics do not normally reach d20.

A specific ability can nonetheless temporarily or permanently increase a statistic, respecting its description.

13.3 · The starting ship

An initial ship normally has:

Initial configuration

One statistic at d8.

The other three statistics at d6.

The crew chooses which statistic to raise to d8 in agreement with the Game Master.

A ship designed to withstand encounters could begin with d8 Hull.

A crew interested in speed could instead choose d8 Sails.

The default configuration in the Ship Portal uses d8 Hull and the other statistics at d6, but represents only an initial model.

13.4 · Structure Points

Structure Points (SP) represent the ship’s resilience.

They function like a character’s Hit Points, but belong exclusively to the vessel.

The maximum value depends on the Hull die.

HullMaximum SP
d420
d630
d840
d1050
d1260

A Module or a specific ability can modify the maximum.

Maximum Structure Points

Hull SP + Modifiers

When a ship takes damage, it is subtracted from its current SP.

When repaired, it recovers SP up to its maximum, unless effects specify different conditions.

The ship’s SP are not transferred to characters.

A cannon shot can damage the hull without automatically dealing the same damage to every pirate on deck.

The GM determines any consequences for characters exposed to the explosion separately.

13.5 · Ship conditions

Conditions depend on the percentage of current SP relative to the maximum.

Hull conditions

Intact

Over 75%

The ship is in operational condition.

Damaged

Over 50% up to 75%

The hull has significant damage.

Severe

Over 25% up to 50%

The ship risks losing essential components.

Critical

Over 0% up to 25%

A component may fail. The ship is close to sinking.

Sunk

0 SP

The ship can no longer continue normal navigation or combat.

The condition updates when SP change.

A ship can therefore go from Severe to Critical after a cannon shot, or improve its condition through repairs.

Some Modules modify sinking rules. Watertight Compartments, for example, allows the ship to withstand going below 0 SP and sink only at −10 SP.

This ability is an exception to the ordinary rule.

13.6 · Damage to components

A ship is not a single block of Structure Points.

When a ship enters Critical condition for the first time during a naval battle, roll 1d6 on the Components table.

d6ComponentConsequence
1MainmastSails −1 die and speed halved.
2RudderYou cannot perform an Evasive Manoeuvre.
3Cannon batteryArtillery −1 die.
4HoldCargo damaged or lost.
5Engine roomThe ship cannot move actively.
6Hull below the waterlineMaximum SP −10 permanently, until the damage is repaired.

The indicated component suffers the specified consequence.

Remaining in Critical condition does not trigger new automatic rolls each round.

If the ship is repaired above the Critical threshold and later enters it again due to new damage, make a new roll.

A Clean Hit against the hull does not automatically damage a component.

An attack can hit a component directly only if:

  • An ability expressly permits it.
  • The component is physically exposed and the Game Master considers it possible to target.

Component already damaged

If the d6 indicates a component already damaged, reroll once.

If the second result also indicates a component already out of order, the Game Master can:

  • Worsen its condition consistently.
  • Or apply the damage to another exposed component.

Reducing Maximum SP

If damage reduces the ship’s Maximum SP and Current SP exceed the new maximum, Current SP immediately fall to the new maximum value.

If they were already lower, they remain unchanged.

13.7 · Crew stations

During a naval battle, characters take up stations.

Each station uses a specific Attribute and contributes to an aspect of navigation or combat.

StationAttributeTask
CaptainSpiritCoordinate and support allies.
HelmsmanCunningNavigation and manoeuvres.
GunnerTechniqueUse Artillery.
LookoutCunningSpot dangers and ambushes.
